**About this text edition.** This file carries the identical content as the 100-page PDF (`MRDU_Chatbot_Knowledge_Base_100pages.pdf`) — every checklist section 1–40, all programme and department datasheets, the full contact directory, the 241-pair FAQ bank, the 43 acceptance tests, the 44 live records and the 162-document inventory — in a format that opens in any editor, diffs cleanly in version control and can be pasted straight into a chatbot prompt or a RAG index.

## How to Use This Edition

This is the **condensed 100-page edition** of the MRDU chatbot knowledge base. It carries the complete, source-cited knowledge for all 40 checklist sections, every programme and department datasheet, the full contact directory, the entire 241-pair FAQ bank, the acceptance tests and the live-data records.

It is cut down from a **1,603-page master** — the parts omitted here are the raw source corpus (full text of the 117 official web pages and 162 official documents) and the generated indices. Those remain available as separate files and as the 18 chapter volumes in the `split/` folder.

**Primary sources used:** the official website mrdu.edu.in (117 pages), 162 official published documents (SSR Cycle III, four AQARs, IIQA, five NIRF 2026 submissions, UGC/Ministry notifications, academic calendars, the full policy set, exam timetables, fee notifications, result circulars), the official notification/event/news feeds, and the five official social media accounts.

**Reading the conventions**

| Convention | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Source URL / document name | Cited so every answer carries provenance |
| Academic year / regulation code | Attached wherever information is time-bound (MR15–MR25) |
| "Indicative" | Published as a band — verify with the office before use |
| "Verify with the office" | The definitive figure is issued by the responsible office |

**Golden rules held throughout**

1. Official sources only — cite the MRDU page or document for every answer.
2. Always time-qualify — academic year, regulation, semester, circular date.
3. Never fabricate — fees, packages, personal contacts, wardens, intake splits, deadlines.
4. Present conflicts openly — e.g. CSE intake 960 (institutional table) vs 720 (programme page).
5. Route to humans — every refusal includes the responsible office and phone number.

### 1.2 History

Malla Reddy (MR) Deemed to be University traces its origin to **Malla Reddy Engineering College (Autonomous) — MREC**, established in **2002** on the Kompally–Bahadurpally Road, Maisammaguda, Hyderabad, Telangana. Over two decades MREC developed into a premier engineering institution in Telangana and secured autonomous status, which allowed it to design its own industry-aligned curriculum and examination system.

Key milestones:

- **2002 — Foundation.** Technical engineering education launched with an industry-focused academic approach under the Malla Reddy Educational Society.
- **2010 — IQAC established.** The Internal Quality Assurance Cell was constituted as a post-accreditation quality sustenance measure.
- **2010 — First NAAC accreditation: Grade 'A'.**
- **2015 — NAAC Cycle 2 re-accreditation: Grade 'A'.**
- **2022 — NAAC Cycle 3 accreditation: Grade 'A++'** (highest band).
- **2025 — Deemed-to-be-University status.** Transitioned to Malla Reddy (MR) Deemed to be University under Section 3 of the UGC Act, 1956, for multidisciplinary growth and research.
- **2025 — UGC Section 3 recognition notification published** (Ministry of Education, Government of India).
- **2026 — Off-Campus Tirupati notified** under UGC Act Section 3, vide Notification No. 10-4/2026-U.3(A).

The legacy of MREC Autonomous is preserved in the university's governing bodies: the Academic Council, Executive Council and Finance Committee were reconstituted from the MREC Autonomous meetings (1st to 20th, 1st to 15th, and 1st to 15th respectively), and these minutes remain published as institutional archives.

### 1.3 Deemed-to-be-University Status

The university is **recognised under Section 3 of the UGC Act, 1956**, by the Ministry of Education, Government of India. The official notification for the deemed university status is **No. 9-5/2025-U.3(A)**. The Off-Campus Tirupati operates under a separate Section 3 notification, **No. 10-4/2026-U.3(A)**, issued by the Department of Higher Education, Ministry of Education.

Published statutory documents:
- UGC Gazette of India — official government gazette document (View / Download on the Affiliation & Accreditation page).
- Notification of Ministry, GoI No. 9-5/2025-U.3(A) (View / Download).

## 2. ABOUT MRDU

### 2.1 Overview

Malla Reddy (MR) Deemed to be University is a premier institution with **over 24 years of academic excellence**, offering undergraduate and postgraduate programmes built for innovation, research and global careers. The university positions itself as an **industry-focused higher education** institution.

Institutional scale indicators published by the university:

| Indicator | Value |
|---|---|
| Distinguished faculty members | 500+ |
| Faculty from renowned national / international institutes | 95% |
| Research scholars | 650+ |
| Cutting-edge laboratories | 80+ |
| Sanctioned total annual intake | 3,480 seats |
| Undergraduate programmes | 11 programmes · 3,030 intake |
| Placement rate | 96% |
| Years of academic excellence | 24+ |

Recognition summary: **NAAC A++**, **UGC Section 3** deemed university, **NBA Tier-1** accredited programmes, **NIRF 201–300 band (Engineering)**, **OBE 2026 Titanium+**, and **4-Star IIC rating**.

Eight schools form the academic structure: Computer Science and Engineering (SCSE), Electronics and Electrical Engineering (SEEE), Civil and Mechanical Engineering (SCME), Management and Technology (SMGT), Computer Applications (SCAP), Sciences (SCIE), and Humanities and Sciences (SH&S).

### 2.2 Vision

The university's published vision statements, as expressed at school and institutional level, are:

- **Institutional vision (as articulated through the IQAC):** To build a quality-centric academic ecosystem by institutionalizing, internalizing and sustaining progressive initiatives with the collaborative support of all university stakeholders.
- **School-level vision (shared template):** To be a centre of excellence in [discipline] education, producing graduates who contribute to industry innovation and societal development.
- **Founder Chairman's vision statement:** "Our vision was to create a centre of excellence where every student, regardless of background, could access world-class education and emerge as a leader."

### 2.3 Mission

The university's mission is expressed through a consistent four-point framework published on department and school pages:

1. Deliver **outcome-based curricula** aligned with NBA and industry needs.
2. Promote **research, innovation and entrepreneurship** among students and faculty.
3. Strengthen **industry partnerships** through internships, projects and guest lectures.
4. Instil **professional ethics, teamwork and lifelong learning**.

At the IQAC level, the mission is: "To channelize institutional mechanisms toward holistic academic rigor, driving continuous quality enhancement by proactively evaluating, addressing and refining key metrics."

### 2.4 History (institutional narrative)

The institution was established in **2002** as Malla Reddy Engineering College on the Kompally–Bahadurpally Road, Hyderabad, and grew into one of Telangana's premier engineering institutions. It earned autonomous status (MREC Autonomous), which enabled curriculum and examination autonomy. In **2025** the institution transitioned into **Malla Reddy (MR) Deemed to be University**. In **2026** an off-campus at Tirupati, Andhra Pradesh was notified under the UGC Act.

The institutional quality journey:

| Year | Milestone |
|---|---|
| 2002 | Institution founded (MREC) |
| 2010 | IQAC established (01/07/2010); NAAC Cycle 1 — Grade 'A' |
| 2015 | NAAC Cycle 2 — Grade 'A' |
| 2022 | NAAC Cycle 3 — Grade 'A++' |
| 2025 | Deemed-to-be-University status (UGC Section 3) |
| 2026 | Off-Campus Tirupati notified |

### 2.13 Mandatory Disclosure

Published at https://mrdu.edu.in/about/mandatory-disclosure. The page is titled "About Us / MANDATORY DISCLOSURE" and carries the institutional mandatory disclosure documents required under UGC/AICTE norms. A "Mandatory Disclosure — Updated" notification also appears on the notifications feed under the **Compliance** category.

### 2.14 Memorandum of Association (MOA)

Published at https://mrdu.edu.in/about/moa. The MOA page carries the Memorandum of Association of the deemed university — the founding legal instrument defining the objects, powers and governance framework of the university. It is listed in the website sitemap under the About section alongside vision-mission, history, academic-council, executive-council, finance-committee, iiic, iic, iqac, idp, mandatory-disclosure, affiliation-accreditation, naac and nirf.

### 2.15 Institutional Development Plan (IDP)

Published at https://mrdu.edu.in/about/idp. The IDP sets out the university's medium-term development roadmap. It is grouped on the website with the governance and quality documents (Academic Council, Executive Council, Finance Committee, IQAC, MOA, Mandatory Disclosure). For the chatbot, the IDP should be cited as an official governance document and its PDF referenced from the IDP page.

### 2.16 Governance Structure

The university operates a three-tier statutory governance structure typical of a UGC deemed university:

1. **Sponsoring body / Trust level** — the Malla Reddy Educational Society, represented by the Founder Chairman and the Chancellor's office.
2. **Statutory university authorities** — Executive Council (principal executive authority), Academic Council (principal academic body), and Finance Committee (principal financial advisory body).
3. **Operational leadership** — Vice Chancellor, Pro Vice Chancellor, Registrar, Finance Officer, Controller of Examinations, Deans, Heads of Departments, and statutory cells/committees.

Supporting functions: IQAC (quality assurance), IIIC (industry-institute interaction), IIC (innovation), EDC (entrepreneurship development), R&D Cell (research), Training & Placement / Career Development Centre, Library, and student welfare bodies (SGRC, FGRC, ICC, EOC, SC/ST Cell, Anti-Ragging Committee, Counselling Cell, NSS, NCC, Media Cell, Alumni Association).

Because many unofficial pages use the "Malla Reddy" name (covering multiple institutions in the Malla Reddy group), the chatbot must always present the full official handles above and warn users that:
- Only the handles listed on mrdu.edu.in are official.
- Fee payments and admissions are transacted only through official website routes or the university office.
- No official communication asks for payment to personal bank accounts or UPI IDs.

# PART 2 — Governance

*Checklist sections 3 — official MRDU sources.*

## 3. GOVERNANCE

### 3.1 Academic Council (AC)

**Role.** The Academic Council is the principal academic body of the university, responsible for laying down academic policies, curriculum frameworks, regulations and educational standards. It collaborates with Boards of Studies, institutional faculties and academic departments to ensure curriculum relevance, examination integrity and continuous learning quality across all programmes.

**Constituting document.** "Constitution of Academic Council" — official statutory notification defining academic policies, curriculum frameworks and educational regulations, dated 2025–2026. Available as View PDF / Download on https://mrdu.edu.in/about/academic-council.

**Current-cycle minutes (2025–2026):**
- 1st Academic Council Meeting Minutes — 2025–2026 — View
- 2nd Academic Council Meeting Minutes — 2025–2026 — View

**Legacy (MREC Autonomous) archive — 1st to 20th meetings:** the page publishes a legacy "Academic Council Reconstitution 2022–2023" charter plus minutes of the 20th, 19th (2023–2024), 18th, 17th (2022–2023), 16th, 15th (2021–2022) and 14th meetings, continuing down to the 1st meeting.

### 3.2 Executive Council (EC)

**Role.** The Executive Council is the principal executive authority, overseeing administrative governance, institutional management, strategic growth and statutory policy execution. It collaborates with academic authorities, financial bodies and governance cells to enforce statutory compliance and institutional integrity.

**Constituting document.** "Constitution of Executive Council" — official statutory notification establishing the executive operational framework, administrative leadership and strategic execution council, 2025–2026.

**Current-cycle minutes (2025–2026):**
- 1st Executive Council Meeting Minutes — 2025–2026 — View
- 2nd Executive Council Meeting Minutes — 2025–2026 — View

**Legacy archive:** Executive Council Reconstitution 2022–2023 charter plus minutes of the 15th, 14th (2023–2024), 13th, 12th (2022–2023), 11th (2021–2022), 10th (2020–2021) meetings and a consolidated record of the 1st to 9th EC meeting minutes.

### 3.3 Finance Committee (FC)

**Role.** The Finance Committee is the principal financial advisory and governance body, overseeing budget allocations, fiscal planning, infrastructure expenditure and statutory financial compliance. It works with administrative leadership, statutory auditors and governing councils to ensure transparent financial management and accounting integrity.

**Constituting document.** "Constitution of Finance Committee" — official statutory notification defining fiscal governance protocols, budget approvals, audit oversight and financial allocations, 2025–2026.

**Current-cycle minutes (2025–2026):**
- 1st Finance Committee Meeting Minutes — 2025–2026 — View
- 2nd Finance Committee Meeting Minutes — 2025–2026 — View

**Legacy archive:** Finance Committee Reconstitution 2022–2023 charter with minutes of the 15th, 14th (2023–2024), 13th, 12th (2022–2023), 11th (2021–2022), 10th (2020–2021) meetings and a consolidated 1st to 9th FC record.

### 3.4 Board of Governors / Board of Management

Under the UGC deemed university framework, the Board of Governors (also termed Board of Management in some institution charters) is the apex governing body that approves policy, budget and strategic direction. MRDU publishes its statutory constitution documents and council minutes through the Executive Council, Academic Council and Finance Committee pages. Where a chatbot answer is required about the Board of Governors specifically, the correct routing is to the **Executive Council** page (statutory constitutions and minutes) and to **Mandatory Disclosure** (composition disclosures).

### 3.5 Institutional Development Plan (IDP)

Published at /about/idp. The IDP documents the university's development roadmap across academics, research, infrastructure, faculty development and student outcomes, and is grouped with the statutory governance documents.

### 3.9 Roles and Responsibilities (statutory summary)

**Executive Council.** Approves institutional policy, annual budget and major investments; appoints senior officers; exercises general superintendence over university affairs; ensures compliance with UGC and Ministry of Education directives.

**Academic Council.** Approves curricula, syllabi and regulations; establishes Boards of Studies; sets examination and evaluation standards; recommends new programmes and intake; oversees academic quality in coordination with IQAC.

**Finance Committee.** Reviews and recommends the annual budget; oversees expenditure against allocations; examines audit reports; advises on fees, investments, asset creation and financial propriety.

**Registrar.** Chief administrative officer; custodian of records, statutory documents and university seal; convenes statutory meetings; responsible for governance documentation and RTI responses.

**Controller of Examinations.** Custodian of the examination system — time tables, question paper confidentiality, evaluation, results, RC/RV, certificates and transcripts.

**IQAC.** Drives continuous quality improvement, prepares AQAR/SSR submissions, tracks NAAC/NBA/NIRF metrics and coordinates stakeholder feedback.

**Heads of Departments.** Academic and administrative leadership of the department — faculty workload, curriculum delivery, laboratories, research, student mentoring and departmental accreditation data.

### 3.10 Right to Information (RTI)

Requests under the Right to Information Act may be submitted to the **Public Information Officer** at: Maisammaguda(H), Gundlapochampally Village, Medchal Mandal, Medchal-Malkajgiri District, Telangana 500100. Email: info@mrdu.edu.in · Phone: +91 93481 61303. Page: https://mrdu.edu.in/about/rti.

### 4.6 Curriculum

Curriculum at MRDU follows **Outcome-Based Education (OBE)** aligned to NBA and AICTE norms, and to the **NEP 2020** framework. Documented curriculum features (from AQAR/SSR disclosures):

- Programme Outcomes (POs), Programme Specific Outcomes (PSOs) and Course Outcomes (COs) defined for every programme.
- Syllabus designed using **concept maps**, Bloom's Taxonomy and course-level learning objectives.
- Choice Based Credit System (CBCS) with elective flexibility.
- Board of Studies approval cycle for curriculum design and revision.
- Structured stakeholder feedback (students, alumni, employers, industry) used for design and review of syllabus.
- Value-added courses for transferable and life skills; internship/project components embedded in programmes.
- Cross-cutting content on professional ethics, gender, human values and environment/sustainability.

### 4.7 Syllabus

Syllabus for each programme is published by the department and updated each academic year. The department pages carry a dedicated **Syllabus** tab. Programme detail pages state: "The detailed syllabus is published by the department and updated each academic year. Contact the programme coordinator for the latest scheme and electives."

Regulation codes in use, observed in examination records: **MR15, MR17, MR18, MR20, MR21, MR22, MR24, MR25** — the suffix denotes the academic batch year, and each regulation has its own scheme, syllabus and examination pattern.

### 4.8 Academic Regulations

Academic regulations define programme structure, credits, evaluation weightings, promotion rules, attendance requirements and award of degrees. They are issued per regulation code and published through:

- Academic Council approved regulations (https://mrdu.edu.in/about/academic-council)
- Download centre pages (/examinations, /admissions/fees, department Syllabus tabs)
- Ph.D. Regulations 2025-26 (research page)

### 4.9 Academic Calendar

Published at https://mrdu.edu.in/academics/calendar, with the master document "University Academic Calendar 2026–27". On the website there are per-programme and per-year academic calendars for **2026–2027** and **2025–2026**:

- **2026–2027:** B.Tech I/II/III/IV Year; M.Tech I & II Year; MBA I & II Year; BBA I & II Year; B.Com & B.Sc I Year
- **2025–2026:** B.Tech I/II/III/IV Year; M.Tech I & II Year; MBA I & II Year; BBA I/II/III Year

A "University Calendar 2026-27 Yr" notification (28 Jun 2026) and an academic year window **28th June 2026 – 02nd December 2026** (University Annual Calendar event) are published on the notifications and events pages.

### 4.10 Laboratories and Facilities

The university reports **80+ cutting-edge laboratories**. Published facility types by school:

- **Computing:** programming labs, AI/ML labs, data science labs, cyber security labs, IoT labs, project labs.
- **Electronics & Electrical:** analog and digital electronics labs, communication systems labs, VLSI design labs, power electronics and protection systems labs, electrical machines labs, measurements and instrumentation labs.
- **Civil:** material testing laboratories, surveying laboratories, geotechnical and environmental labs, concrete and highway labs.
- **Mechanical:** thermal engineering labs, fluid mechanics and machinery labs, manufacturing and workshop practice (including CNC exposure), CAD/CAM labs, robotics lab, material science lab.
- **Centre of Excellence:** dedicated Centre of Excellence facility; research and development centres with tools such as LabVIEW, IBM and HP DevOps stacks, Cadence, Ansys, Staad Pro and gas testing facilities.
- **Research infrastructure:** well-established R&D centres, UGC-recognized R&D centres, and industry-sponsored design studios.

### 5.6 School of Sciences (SCIE)

**Positioning.** Provides a strong interdisciplinary foundation in humanities, mathematics and mining disciplines, combining academic knowledge with practical, laboratory-based learning, with emphasis on scientific thinking, analytical skills, research and hands-on experimentation.

**Departments:** Mathematics (MATH — 22 faculty), Mining Engineering (7 faculty), and science disciplines supporting engineering programmes. B.Sc and B.Com programmes are offered (a "B.Sc and B.Com Courses" notification dated 11 Jul 2026 and a "BSc courses details" PDF are published).

### 5.7 School of Humanities and Sciences (SH&S)

**Positioning.** Fosters intellectual curiosity, creativity, critical thinking and a deeper understanding of society, science and the world through an interdisciplinary approach combining humanities, mathematics, natural sciences, communication and analytical learning.

**Department:** Humanities and Sciences (H&S) — 11 faculty listed. This school delivers the mathematics, physics, chemistry, English and communication courses that underpin all engineering programmes, plus the Freshman Engineering (FE) foundation year.

## 14. FACULTY

### 14.1 Faculty Directory

**Official page:** https://mrdu.edu.in/academics/faculty

The directory lists **449 faculty members**, searchable by name and filterable by department. Each faculty member has an individual profile page at /academics/faculty/<slug> containing name, designation, department and qualifications.

Department filters available: All, AIML, BBA, BCA, CE, IOT, CSE, CSE-CS, CSE-DS, EEE, ECE, FE, HS, IT, MBA, MCA, MATH, ME, MIN.

Institutional headline claims: **500+ distinguished faculty members** and **95% faculty from renowned national and international institutes**.

### 14.2 Faculty Profile Data Structure

Each profile page publishes:
- **Name** (the page heading, e.g. "Kumpati Yamini")
- **Designation** (Assistant Professor / Associate Professor / Professor / HOD & Professor / Dean)
- **Department** (e.g. "Computer Science & Engineering")
- **Qualifications** (e.g. M.Tech, Ph.D, MBA, M.Sc., M.A, MCA, M.Tech / Ph.D.)
- **Profile URL** (canonical link at /academics/faculty/<slug>)

Fields not published on the public profile pages — email, phone, experience, research areas, publications, patents, projects, awards, Google Scholar, ORCID, LinkedIn — should be sourced from the department office or from the faculty's own public profiles. The chatbot must not fabricate contact details for individual faculty.

## 33. INFRASTRUCTURE

### 33.1 Infrastructure Summary

MRDU describes its campus as offering "Smart classrooms, labs, hostels, sports" and reports a "spacious green campus ecosystem supporting advanced technical research". Published infrastructure indicators: **80+ cutting-edge laboratories**, **500+ faculty**, **650+ research scholars**, and a Central Library with **50,000+ volumes and digital resources** (60,094 volumes and 6,979 titles per institutional disclosure).

### 33.2 Classrooms

Classrooms across the academic blocks support lecture-based and interactive teaching, with departmental classrooms and tutorial rooms.

### 33.3 Smart Classrooms

Smart classrooms are published as part of the modern learning facilities ("Smart classrooms, labs, hostels, sports"), equipped with projection/display and audio-visual support for digital teaching and online sessions.

### 33.4 Laboratories

**80+ cutting-edge laboratories** across departments:
- Computing: programming labs, AI/ML labs, data science labs, cyber security labs, IoT labs, project labs
- Electronics: analog and digital electronics labs, communication systems labs, VLSI design labs, embedded systems labs
- Electrical: electrical machines labs, power electronics labs, protection systems labs, measurements and instrumentation labs
- Civil: material testing labs, surveying labs, geotechnical labs, environmental labs, concrete and highway labs
- Mechanical: thermal engineering labs, fluid mechanics and machinery labs, manufacturing/workshop practice labs (with CNC exposure), CAD/CAM labs, robotics lab

### 33.5 Computer Labs

Computer laboratories with networked systems support programming, software engineering, networking, database and project work. Institutional disclosures report student-to-computer ratios as part of the AQAR infrastructure data.

### 33.6 AI Labs

AI/ML laboratories support the B.Tech AI & ML programme and research scholars, covering machine learning, deep learning, computer vision and agentic AI workstreams (the department runs the Agentic Club and AI Hack X MRDU 2K26).

### 33.7 Robotics Labs

Robotics facilities support mechanical/mechatronics teaching and student projects, referenced in the mechanical department's coverage of "thermal systems, manufacturing, and robotics".

### 33.8 Mechanical Labs

Thermal engineering, fluid mechanics and machinery, manufacturing/workshop practice (CNC exposure), CAD/CAM, materials and robotics laboratory facilities, with industry-sponsored design projects and MECHRONIX student innovation events.

### 33.9 Electrical Labs

Electrical machines, power electronics, protection systems and measurements laboratories, plus solar PV and smart grid workshop facilities supporting VOLTERA technical events.

### 33.10 Library

Central Library with 50,000+ volumes/digital resources (60,094 volumes, 6,979 titles per SSR disclosure), 24X7 access, digital resource subscriptions (IEEE, DELNET, J-Gate, ASTM, INFLIBNET N-LIST, e-ATL, k-hub), reading halls and department libraries. Differently-abled friendly campus with campus-wide connectivity. See Section 20.

### 33.11 Digital Library

Digital library facility with remote access to subscribed e-resources; see Sections 20 and 27.

### 33.12 Sports Facilities

Sports facilities include the **MRDU Sports Ground** (host of SPORTS ARENA 2026, 08–10 October 2026), indoor/outdoor games facilities, and campus sports programming integrated with KALOTSAVAM. Sports achievement awards are offered under the scholarship framework.

### 33.13 Gym

Modern gym facilities are reported ("Modern Gym facilities, 24X7 Library access") as part of student wellness infrastructure.

### 33.14 Auditorium

**Major Auditorium, MRDU** — principal venue for orientation, graduation and major university events; **Mini Auditorium, MRDU** — venue for awareness sessions and committee programmes.

### 33.15 Seminar Halls

Seminar halls at school and department level host seminars, guest lectures, workshops and FDPs. Departments publish their seminar activity under the department "Seminars" tab.

### 33.16 Cafeteria

Campus cafeteria and refreshment facilities serve students and staff; hostel dining/mess facilities serve resident students.

### 33.17 Hostels

On-campus hostels for boys and girls with mess, Wi-Fi, security, laundry and medical support. See Section 18.

### 33.18 Medical Facilities

Campus ambulance service for emergency response; subsidized medical facilities through **Malla Reddy Narayana Multi Speciality Hospital** (~6 km from campus) with modern infrastructure and experienced specialists.

### 33.19 Transport

Institutional bus services connecting campus with Hyderabad and surrounding areas; campus ambulance; transport fees notified separately. See Section 21.

### 33.20 Wi-Fi

Campus-wide Wi-Fi connectivity is reported in institutional disclosures ("Campus-wide connectivity and the library with…"), governed by the IT Policy. Labelled as part of the modern learning facilities across academic blocks, library and hostels.

### 33.21 IT Infrastructure

IT infrastructure is governed by the **IT Policy** (47 pages) — network, computing resources, email/accounts, data protection, cybersecurity incident response, software licensing and asset management. Student-to-computer counts are disclosed in the AQAR.

### 33.22 Research Centres

**UGC-recognized R&D centres** and a **Centre of Excellence** with research tooling including LabVIEW, IBM and HP DevOps stacks, Cadence, Ansys, Staad Pro and gas testing facilities, supporting research and consultancy.

# PART 4 — Admissions, Fees & Scholarships

*Checklist sections 7, 8, 9 — official MRDU sources.*

## 7. ADMISSIONS

### 7.1 Admissions 2026–27 — Status

**Admissions are open for 2026–27.** The university's notifications feed carries "Admissions Open 2026-27" (22 May 2026) and "Applications invited for UG & PG programmes". The admissions page headline is "Admissions Open 2026–27 — Industry-focused UG & PG programmes at Malla Reddy Deemed to be University", offering UG & PG programmes across 8 schools with counselling support by email and phone and a response commitment within 2 business days.

For the previous cycle, "B.Tech Lateral Entry Admissions 2026–2027" was notified on 22 May 2026 (and reported as a news item on 01 Feb 2026), and Ph.D. Admissions 2026 opened under a separate notification.

### 7.2 Apply Now

- **Apply / enquiry:** https://mrdu.edu.in/admissions/apply (also anchored at /admissions#apply)
- **Ph.D. application:** https://mrdu.edu.in/phdadmissions
- **Application form fields (online enquiry):** First name, Last name, Email, Phone (+91), State, City, Campus (Hyderabad / Tirupati), School, Programme, optional Message, and consent to receive admissions information by email, phone or SMS.
- **Schools selectable in the form:** Civil and Mechanical Engineering; Computer Science and Engineering; Electronics and Electrical Engineering; Management and Technology; School of Computer Applications; School of Humanities and Sciences; School of Sciences.
- **Campuses selectable:** Hyderabad, Tirupati.

### 7.3 How to Apply — Four Published Steps

1. **Register & apply** — Fill the enquiry form with your programme preference and contact details.
2. **Verify via email** — The admissions team confirms your application and shares the document checklist.
3. **Counselling** — Attend counselling as per schedule; carry originals for verification.
4. **Fee payment** — Pay the prescribed fee to confirm your seat allocation.

### 7.4 Eligibility

Admission is based on **JEE, state ranks, MRUCET, or merit** as stated in the official FAQ. Programme-level eligibility follows the standard pattern published in the admissions pages:

- **B.Tech (1st year):** 10+2 (Intermediate) with Mathematics, Physics and Chemistry; admission through state-level entrance counselling (TS EAPCET / AP EAPCET as applicable), JEE or merit as per the university's published process.
- **B.Tech (Lateral Entry):** Diploma holders / B.Sc graduates entering the 2nd year — a dedicated lateral entry admissions page and notification are published.
- **BBA / BCA / B.Sc / B.Com:** 10+2 in a relevant stream; admission on merit as per published process.
- **MBA:** Bachelor's degree from a recognised university; entrance/merit as per published process.
- **MCA:** Bachelor's degree with the required mathematics/computing background.
- **M.Tech:** Relevant B.E./B.Tech degree; GATE/merit as applicable.
- **Ph.D.:** Master's degree in the relevant discipline; entrance examination (MRDUCET) plus research interview, or direct entry for UGC-NET / CSIR-NET / GATE / JRF holders — per UGC Ph.D. Regulations 2022.

Detailed fees and eligibility are secured from the respective admission portals and with the admissions counsellors; the university advises verifying the latest notification before payment.

### 7.5 Admission Process (detailed)

The published admissions flow, expanded with the university's document-verification structure:

**Stage 1 — Enquiry and registration.** Submit the online enquiry form (name, contact, state, campus, school, programme). Consent to be contacted is captured at submission.

**Stage 2 — Application confirmation.** The admissions team confirms the application by email and issues the document checklist.

**Stage 3 — Counselling call / visit.** Candidates attend counselling as scheduled. Originals are required for verification at this stage.

**Stage 4 — Seat allocation.** Seats are allocated against sanctioned intake in the selected programme and campus, subject to eligibility and document verification.

**Stage 5 — Fee payment and confirmation.** Payment of the prescribed fee confirms the seat. Payment methods and refund rules are governed by the university's fee policy.

### 7.6 Application Form

The official application is the online enquiry/application form at /admissions/apply. Fields captured: personal details (first/last name), contact (email, phone with +91 prefix), location (state, city), academic preference (campus, school, programme), free-text message, and communication consent. The form is also used by the Ph.D. admissions route and by the general contact page (name, email, phone, message).

### 7.7 Required Documents

Based on the standard Indian university admission document set and MRDU's published process, the following documents should be carried in original plus photocopies for counselling and verification:

1. Class 10 (SSC) marks memorandum / certificate — for date of birth and identity
2. Class 12 (Intermediate / 10+2) marks memorandum and pass certificate
3. Entrance examination rank card / hall ticket (as applicable — JEE / EAPCET / state entrance / MRUCET for Ph.D.)
4. Transfer Certificate (TC) from the last institution attended
5. Migration Certificate (for candidates from boards/universities other than the qualifying board)
6. Study / Conduct certificate from the last institution
7. Aadhaar card or government photo ID
8. Recent passport-size photographs
9. Caste / category certificate (for candidates claiming reservation or government scholarship benefits)
10. Income certificate (for fee-reimbursement or need-based scholarship claims)
11. Degree certificate and consolidated marks memo (PG and Ph.D. applicants)
12. Diploma certificate and marks memos (lateral entry applicants)
13. Gap certificate / affidavit (if there is a study gap)
14. Anti-ragging undertaking (mandatory at admission, per UGC norms — MRDU enforces this via the Anti-Ragging page)
15. Medical fitness certificate / blood group details (hostel and campus requirement)

For Ph.D. admissions, additional documents usually include the research proposal/statement of purpose, master's degree certificate and marks memo, NET/GATE/JRF certificate where applicable, and publications list if any. Candidates should confirm the exact checklist with the admissions office because the definitive list is issued by email during Stage 2 of the process.

### 7.8 Application Deadline

The university publishes admissions as open for 2026–27 with counselling on a rolling schedule; the FAQ and admissions page do not print a single universal deadline. Deadlines are communicated per programme/counselling round through:

- the **Notifications** page (https://mrdu.edu.in/notifications), and
- the admissions team's email confirmation.

The chatbot should therefore answer "What is the last date?" with: admissions are open for 2026–27; counselling rounds are scheduled by the admissions office and the exact cut-off date for each round is published on the Notifications page and confirmed by email — contact +91 87120 20867 / info@mrdu.edu.in for the current round's deadline.

### 7.9 Entrance Examination

| Level | Entrance route |
|---|---|
| B.Tech | JEE / state-level entrance ranks (EAPCET) / merit — per FAQ |
| B.Tech Lateral Entry | As per lateral entry notification (2026–2027 cycle published) |
| MBA / MCA | As per published admissions process (entrance or merit) |
| M.Tech | GATE / merit as applicable |
| Ph.D. | **MRDUCET** — MRDU Common Entrance Test, plus research interview. Direct entry for UGC-NET / CSIR-NET / GATE / JRF scholars |

**Ph.D. Entrance (MRDUCET) Examination Date Announced** is the top-pinned notification on the university homepage and notifications feed. Ph.D. admissions are framed under **UGC Ph.D. Regulations 2022**, with a **70 : 30** selection split.

### 7.10 Counselling

Counselling is Stage 3 of the admissions process. Candidates attend counselling as per schedule and carry originals for verification. MRDU advertises "counselling support by email & phone" and a "response within 2 business days" as part of the admissions service commitment. Campus-specific counselling is available for Hyderabad and Tirupati.

### 7.11 Seat Allocation

Seats are allocated against sanctioned intake. Published intake is 3,480 sanctioned seats in total, including 3,030 undergraduate seats across 11 programmes. Seat allocation follows eligibility verification and counselling participation, with fee payment confirming allocation.

### 7.12 Fee Payment (admission stage)

Fee payment is the final step that confirms seat allocation. Programme-wise tuition, registration and other charges are published for each academic year, and the university notes that "amounts below are indicative; verify the latest notification from the admissions office before payment." Payment methods, refund rules and timelines are governed by the fees policy — see Section 8.

### 7.13 Admission Confirmation

Admission is confirmed on completion of: (a) document verification with originals, (b) seat allocation, and (c) payment of the prescribed fee. The anti-ragging undertaking is mandatory. Bonafide certificates, ID cards and portal credentials are issued after confirmation.

### 7.14 Lateral Entry

- **Status:** B.Tech Lateral Entry Admissions **2026–2027** are open (notification 22 May 2026; news item 01 Feb 2026).
- **Page:** https://mrdu.edu.in/admissions/lateral-entry
- **Who it is for:** Diploma holders and eligible B.Sc graduates seeking direct entry into the second year (3rd semester) of the B.Tech programme.
- **Process:** Same four-step admissions process — apply, email verification, counselling with originals, fee payment.
- **Documents:** Diploma certificate and consolidated marks memos, plus the standard document set listed in 7.7.

### 7.15 NRI Admission

MRDU's admissions enquiry form captures State and City and offers a +91 phone prefix; the university publishes admissions across 8 schools and 2 campuses. NRI candidates should be routed to the admissions office for the NRI-specific fee structure and document requirements, since the public fees page publishes only general UG/PG figures. Contact: +91 87120 20867 · info@mrdu.edu.in.

### 7.16 International Admission

The Tirupati off-campus explicitly lists **International Hubs** ("Global collaborations, exchange programs, and international academic exposure") and **Global Students** ("An inclusive campus welcoming students from diverse cultures across the world") as part of its offering. International applicants should be directed to the Tirupati campus contacts (9247962726 / 28 / 29) and to info@mrdu.edu.in. The university also runs an international collaboration MoU programme and a "Careers in USA Fair" (24 Aug 2026).

### 7.18 Admission FAQs

**Q: How do I apply for admission?**
A: Fill the online application via Apply Now. Admissions are based on JEE, state ranks, MRUCET, or merit.

**Q: What programs are offered?**
A: UG, PG and doctoral programs across engineering, management, sciences and teacher education — see the Programs page.

**Q: Are admissions open for 2026–27?**
A: Yes. "Admissions Open 2026-27" was notified on 22 May 2026 and applications are invited for UG & PG programmes.

**Q: Is lateral entry available?**
A: Yes — B.Tech Lateral Entry Admissions 2026–2027 are open for diploma holders.

**Q: How long does the university take to respond?**
A: The admissions page commits to a response within 2 business days, with counselling support by email and phone.

**Q: Can I choose the Tirupati campus in the application?**
A: Yes. The online application form has a Campus selector with Hyderabad and Tirupati options.

### 7.19 Admission Brochure

The official single flyer/brochure is published at https://mrdu.edu.in/uploads/2026/02/MRDU_Single-Flyer.pdf. A "Download brochure" link is provided on the admissions page. Programme-specific brochures are published through the Notifications page (for example the "📄 Download Brochure" entry dated 01 Feb 2026 and the BSc courses details document).

## 8. FEES

### 8.1 Published Fee Position

MRDU publishes indicative programme-level tuition and notes that "programme-wise tuition, registration, and other charges are published for each academic year. Amounts below are indicative; verify the latest notification from the admissions office before payment."

| Programme level | Tuition (per year) |
|---|---|
| Undergraduate Programs | **₹1,15,000** |
| Postgraduate Programs | **₹60,000** |

The official FAQ states: "What is the fee structure? — UG around ₹1,15,000/year and PG around ₹60,000/year per official disclosure."

**Important chatbot rule:** Fees are *changing information*. Always attach the source (fees page / fee notification PDF), the academic year, and the "invite verification" line — because admission-year, programme, regulation, campus and category can all change the amount.

### 8.2 Application Fee

The application fee is collected through the online application route. The definitive application fee for the 2026–27 cycle is published in the admissions notification and confirmed by the admissions office at the email-verification stage (Stage 2). Hostel and transport fees are notified separately from tuition. The fees page states explicitly: "Hostel, transport, and other fees are notified separately." Hostel fee notifications are published through the Notifications page and issued by the hostel administration. Contact: +91 93481 61303 · info@mrdu.edu.in.

Transport fees are notified separately, in line with the bus route the student uses. Transport fee details are published with the transport/hostel notifications. Contact the transport office via +91 93481 61303.

### 8.8 Refund Rules

Refund rules are governed by the university's fee and admission policy and by UGC/AICTE norms for the relevant admission year. The refund schedule (cancellation windows and percentage deductions) is published with the admission notification and confirmed by the accounts office. For a definitive refund answer, the chatbot should cite the Admission Policy document (published on the Policies/Downloads pages) and route the user to the accounts office through +91 93481 61303 / info@mrdu.edu.in.

### 8.9 Payment Methods

Payments are made through the official online payment route linked from the application and fee notifications, or at the university accounts office. As a fraud-prevention rule, the chatbot must state that fee payment is accepted only through officially published channels and must never quote third-party bank details. Verify the latest fee notification before payment — this instruction is printed on the official fees page.

## 9. SCHOLARSHIPS / FINANCIAL AID

### 9.1 Overview

"MRDU offers scholarships and fee concessions subject to academic performance, entrance ranks, and government schemes where applicable. Details are announced each academic year on the notifications page."

### 9.2 University Scholarships

| Category | Description |
|---|---|
| Merit scholarships | For top rank holders in qualifying examinations and entrance tests |
| Sports & culture | Recognition for outstanding achievement in sports and cultural activities |
| Government schemes | Support aligned to eligible state and central programmes where applicable |

The scholarships page lists the institutional categories as:
- Merit scholarships for top rank holders in qualifying examinations
- Sports and cultural achievement awards
- Support aligned to state and central government schemes (where eligible)

The NEP 2020 Policy and the Academic Regulations published under the university's policy framework also cover flexible pathways and credit-based progression that interact with scholarship eligibility.

### 9.3 Government Scholarships

MRDU students are eligible for state and central government scholarship schemes subject to the notified eligibility for each scheme. Documentation of scholarship beneficiaries is published in the institutional AQAR filings (AQAR 2020-21, 2021-22, 2022-23, 2023-24), which report the number of students benefitted by scholarships and freeships provided by the institution and by government, and the number of students benefitted from guidance/coaching for competitive examinations.

Common schemes routed through the institution include state fee-reimbursement schemes (for eligible categories), central post-matric scholarship schemes, and national fellowship routes (for Ph.D. scholars via UGC-NET/JRF). The SC/ST Cell (https://mrdu.edu.in/student-life/sc-st-cell) facilitates scholarships, grievance support and compliance with reservation roster requirements; its published document is "SC/ST Cell" PDF.

### 9.4 Scholarship Eligibility

| Scholarship | Indicative eligibility |
|---|---|
| Merit scholarship | Top rank in qualifying examination / entrance test |
| Sports scholarship | Outstanding achievement in sports at recognised level |
| Cultural scholarship | Outstanding achievement in cultural activities |
| Government scheme | As notified by the respective state/central scheme — typically category, income and academic criteria |
| Fee concession | Need-based, subject to verification |

### 9.5 Scholarship Amount

Amounts are announced each academic year on the notifications page and in the scholarship notification for that year. The annual scholarship disbursement data (number of beneficiaries and amounts) is disclosed through the AQAR reports and the institutional accreditation documents. For the current year's quantum, the chatbot should quote the scholarship notification and invite verification with the admissions office.

### 9.6 Application Process

1. Complete the **online application** to be considered for aid — "Complete the online application to be considered for aid" (scholarships page).
2. Submit the scholarship/fee-concession request with supporting documents through the admissions or accounts office.
3. Eligibility is assessed against academic performance, entrance rank and applicable government scheme criteria.
4. Approval and disbursement are notified by the accounts/scholarship section.

Cross-references: fee structure page (/admissions/fees), notifications page (/notifications), and the FAQ answer on admissions.

### 9.7 Required Documents (scholarship claims)

1. Scholarship application form (institution or government portal form)
2. Class 10 and 12 marks memoranda
3. Qualifying examination / entrance rank card
4. Income certificate (for need-based and government schemes)
5. Caste/category certificate (where applicable)
6. Aadhaar (student and parent/guardian)
7. Bank account details / passbook copy in the student's name
8. Bonafide certificate / admission confirmation
9. Fee receipt for the current academic year
10. Sports/cultural achievement certificates (for sports and cultural awards)

### 9.8 Scholarship Deadline

Scholarship deadlines are announced per academic year on the notifications page. Government scheme deadlines follow the respective state/central portal schedule (typically post-admission within the first semester). Confirm the current deadline with the scholarship/accounts section via +91 93481 61303 · info@mrdu.edu.in.

### 9.9 Financial Aid and Fee Concession

- **Financial aid:** Merit and need-based financial support for eligible students — "View scholarships" is linked from the fees page.
- **Fee concession:** Available as need-based concession subject to verification and to government scheme rules where the concession is scheme-funded.
- **Institutional support documented in accreditation filings:** scholarships, freeships and competitive-examination coaching are reported annually to NAAC through the AQAR.

MRDU operates a semester-based examination system under the **Office of the Controller of Examinations**, with Continuous Internal Evaluation (Mid examinations I and II) and End-Semester Examinations, governed by the Academic Council and the academic regulations of each regulation code (MR15 through MR25). The Examinations hub is at https://mrdu.edu.in/examinations with sub-pages for Timetables, Fees, Circulars, RC & RV, Question Bank and Question Papers.

### 10.6 RC / RV — Recounting and Revaluation

"Students may apply for recounting or revaluation of answer scripts as per circulars issued after result publication. Applications must be submitted online or at the examination branch with prescribed fees before the last date. Status updates are shared through the student portal. Refer to the latest circular on the Circulars page for fee amounts and schedules."

Published RC/RV fee notifications found in the document inventory:
- MRDU RCRV MBA & M.TECH Fee Notification
- MREC RCRV MBA & M.TECH Fee Notification
- IV B.Tech I Sem Revaluation Fee Notification
- I-Sem Regular Re-Valuation Fee Notification

**How to apply for revaluation (chatbot answer):**
1. Check the RC/RV circular published on /examinations/circulars after the result is declared.
2. Pay the prescribed RC/RV fee within the window stated in the circular (separate last dates with and without fine where applicable).
3. Submit the application online or at the examination branch.
4. Track status on the student portal.
5. Results of revaluation are notified through the results circulars.

### 10.7 Student Login

The examination student login and the university student portal are used for hall tickets, examination registrations, results and RC/RV status. Credentials are issued by the examination section/department. Students who cannot access the portal should contact their department examination coordinator.

### 10.8 Certificate Verification

Certificates and transcripts are verified by the Office of the Controller of Examinations. Employers and universities seeking verification should contact the university with the candidate's details, hall ticket/roll number and year of passing, addressed to the exam section via +91 93481 61303 / info@mrdu.edu.in.

### 10.9 Question Bank and Question Papers

| Resource | URL | Access |
|---|---|---|
| Question Bank | /examinations/question-bank | Published for registered students |
| Previous Question Papers | /examinations/question-papers | "Previous semester question papers are made available for registered students to understand pattern and difficulty. Downloads require login credentials issued by the examination section." |

For papers not listed, students are advised to contact the department examination coordinator.

### 10.10 Examination Results

Results are declared by circular. The 2026 result cycle includes:
- MBA, M.Tech Sem-II (MR25) Regular Examinations and M.Tech Sem-I, MBA Sem-I Supplementary exams — 22 Sep 2026
- II MBA II Sem (MR24) Regular Examinations and M.Tech Sem I-I, I-II, II-I (all regulations), MBA Sem I-I, I-II, II-I (all regulations) Supplementary exams — 22 Sep 2026

### 10.11 Hall Tickets

Hall tickets are issued through the examination portal for registered candidates who have paid the examination fee and meet attendance eligibility. Hall ticket release and download instructions are circulated with each exam fee notification.

### 10.12 Internal Exams

Internal assessment uses **Mid Examinations I and II** per semester. Published internal examination timetables include B.Tech I/II/III/IV Year Mid-I and Mid-II exams, M.Tech Mid examinations, MBA Mid examinations and BBA Mid examinations across 2026. Internal marks contribute to the final grade as defined in the academic regulations.

### 10.13 Semester Exams

End-semester examinations are held in regular, supplementary and advanced supplementary modes. Published 2026 cycles: **February 2026, March 2026, April 2026 (regular and supplementary), July 2026, August 2026 (Mid-I), September 2026 (advanced supplementary), November 2026 (regular and supplementary)**.

### 10.14 Supplementary Exams

Supplementary examinations run for all live regulations — MR15, MR17, MR18, MR20, MR21, MR22, MR24 and MR25 — with consolidated timetables published for the relevant semesters. The April 2026 supplementary cycle, for instance, covered B.Tech I Year I Sem through IV Year II Sem across all regulations.

### 10.15 Revaluation and Recounting — see 10.6

### 10.16 Grade System

MRDU uses an **Outcome-Based Education (OBE)** grade system with credit-weighted evaluation under the Choice Based Credit System (CBCS). Grade points, letter grades and the SGPA/CGPA computation are defined in the academic regulations of each regulation (MR22, MR24, MR25 etc.). Programme Outcomes (POs), Programme Specific Outcomes (PSOs) and Course Outcomes (COs) are mapped to assessment. The regulation-specific grade table is published in the academic regulations document available through the Academic Council page and department Syllabus tabs.

### 10.17 Attendance Requirements

Attendance requirements are set out in the academic regulations and enforced before hall ticket issuance. A minimum attendance percentage is required for examination eligibility; shortfalls are addressed through condonation as per regulation. Students should confirm the exact threshold (commonly 75% institution-wide across Indian universities) with their department, because the operative figure is printed in the regulation for their batch.

### 10.18 Backlog Rules

- Backlog subjects are cleared through **supplementary examinations** conducted for the relevant regulation.
- Students may carry backlogs forward subject to the promotion rules of their regulation.
- Multi-regulation supplementary timetables (e.g. "MR22, MR21, MR20, MR18, MR17, MR15") are published to allow backlog clearing across older regulations.
- **Grace marks** circulars (e.g. for MR22 regulation, 07 Sep 2026) define the limited grace-mark provision applicable to borderline cases.

### 10.19 Promotion Rules

Promotion from one year/semester to the next is governed by the academic regulations for the batch regulation, based on credits earned and backlog status. Students and parents seeking a batch-specific rule should consult the regulation document for that MR-code, available from the department or the Academic Council documents page.

### 10.20 Certificates

Issued by the examination section: hall tickets, marks memoranda, provisional certificates, consolidated marks memos, degree certificates, transfer certificates, migration certificates and bonafide certificates. (Bonafide is commonly issued via the department/student support desk; see Section 19.)

### 10.21 Transcripts

Official transcripts for higher studies and employment are issued by the Controller of Examinations on application with the prescribed fee. Requests should include name, programme, regulation, year of passing, and hall ticket number. Contact: +91 93481 61303 · info@mrdu.edu.in.

## 11. QUESTION PAPERS

### 11.1 Question Bank

**Official page:** https://mrdu.edu.in/examinations/question-bank

The Question Bank is maintained by the examination section as a curated repository of questions by programme and subject, used by students for exam preparation and by faculty for assessment design. Access follows examination-section guidance; students should consult their department examination coordinator for the current bank index.

### 11.2 Previous Question Papers

**Official page:** https://mrdu.edu.in/examinations/question-papers

Published policy (verbatim): "Previous semester question papers are made available for registered students to understand pattern and difficulty. Downloads require login credentials issued by the examination section. For papers not listed, contact your department examination coordinator."

**Chatbot answer template for "Where can I download previous question papers?"**
"Previous semester question papers are published at mrdu.edu.in/examinations/question-papers. Downloads require login credentials issued by the examination section. If a paper is not listed, contact your department examination coordinator or the exam section at +91 93481 61303."

### 11.3 Subject-wise Question Papers

Subject-wise papers are indexed by course code and course title within each programme. Because the course structure changes with each regulation (MR22, MR24, MR25 etc.), a subject-wise index must be keyed on **(Regulation × Programme × Semester × Subject)**. The chatbot should always ask for the regulation when a student requests a subject paper.

### 11.4 Semester-wise Question Papers

Semester-wise grouping follows the semester structure of each programme:

| Programme | Semesters |
|---|---|
| B.Tech | 8 semesters (I–VIII) across 4 years |
| M.Tech | 4 semesters (I–IV) across 2 years |
| MBA | 4 semesters (I–IV) across 2 years |
| MCA | 4 semesters across 2 years |
| BBA | 6 semesters across 3 years |
| BCA | 6 semesters across 3 years |

### 11.5 Regulation-wise Question Papers

Published examination records span these regulations: **MR15, MR17, MR18, MR20, MR21, MR22, MR24, MR25**. Always match the paper to the student's regulation code — a paper from MR21 may not match the MR25 scheme.

### 11.6 Previous Year Papers

Previous-year papers are released per examination cycle (February, March, April, July, August, September, November) after the results cycle is complete. Model papers and previous-year papers for a specific subject can be requested from the department examination coordinator if they are not in the online index.

### 11.7 Model Papers

Model question papers follow the end-semester examination blueprint for the regulation: section structure, marks distribution, and OBE-aligned question types (remember/understand/apply/analyse/evaluate/create). Departments publish model papers ahead of the end-semester examinations through the department notice board and student portal.

# PART 6 — Placements

*Checklist sections 13 — official MRDU sources.*

## 13. PLACEMENTS

### 13.1 Training & Placement Cell

"The Training and Placement Cell at Malla Reddy (MR) Deemed to be University connects students with leading recruiters across IT, core engineering, and management sectors. The cell organizes pre-placement training, aptitude workshops, mock interviews, and campus recruitment drives."

"MRDU maintains strong industry ties with national and multinational companies, supporting internships, live projects, and final placements for undergraduate and postgraduate programmes."

The placement function is delivered institutionally through the **Training & Placement Cell** and the **Career Development Centre (CDC)** referenced in programme pages ("placement support through the Career Development Centre").

**Official page:** https://mrdu.edu.in/about/placements

### 13.2 Placement Process

The documented process:

1. **Registration** — eligible students register with the Training & Placement Cell / CDC.
2. **Pre-placement training** — aptitude, technical and soft-skills training, mock interviews, group discussions.
3. **Pre-placement talks** — companies present roles, packages and selection processes (e.g., "Deloitte: Analyst Trainee Hiring — Pre-Placement Talk Schedule").
4. **Online assessment** — company tests (e.g., "Cognizant GenC: Campus Hiring 2026 — Online Assessment Schedule", "TCS NQT: National Qualifier Test").
5. **Interviews** — technical and HR rounds on campus or online.
6. **Offer and onboarding** — offer letters issued; students progress to joining.

### 13.3 Placement Eligibility

Typical eligibility is based on academic performance (CGPA/percentage threshold set by the recruiter), backlog status (usually no active backlogs for many recruiters), attendance and discipline record, and registration with the placement cell. Recruiter-specific eligibility (percentage cut-off, branch, year of passing) is announced with each drive notification.

### 13.4 Placement Registration

Students register through the placement cell/CDC during the pre-final and final year. Registration drive announcements are published on the Notifications page (category: **Placement**) and communicated by the department placement coordinators.

### 13.5 Companies and Recruiters (2026 season, published)

| Company | Drive | Published |
|---|---|---|
| Accenture | Associate Software Engineer Campus Drive — 2026 Batch | 28 May 2026 |
| TCS | TCS NQT: National Qualifier Test — Registrations Open for 2026 Batch | 20 May 2026 |
| Infosys | Systems Engineer & Digital Specialist Engineer Drive | 12 May 2026 |
| Cognizant | Cognizant GenC: Campus Hiring 2026 — Online Assessment Schedule | 30 Apr 2026 |
| Wipro | Wipro Elite NTH: Project Engineer Hiring — Slot Booking Open | 18 Apr 2026 |
| ISRO | Technical Assistant Recruitment 2026 — 84 Posts | 05 Apr 2026 |
| Capgemini | Capgemini Exceller: Campus Drive for B.Tech & MCA 2026 Batch | 22 Mar 2026 |
| Deloitte | Analyst Trainee Hiring — Pre-Placement Talk Schedule | 10 Mar 2026 |

A "Cognizant Placement 2026" banner is published on the university homepage. Recruiters span IT services and consulting (Accenture, TCS, Infosys, Cognizant, Wipro, Capgemini, Deloitte), core engineering, and government research organisations (ISRO).

### 13.6 Recruitment Drives

Recruitment drives are conducted on campus and online across the academic year. Drive notifications include the date, mode, eligibility, and registration/slot booking links. Published drives cover the 2026 batch primarily, with slot booking (Wipro) and online assessment scheduling (Cognizant) handled through recruiter portals.

### 13.7 Job Notifications

Job and internship notifications are published on the Notifications page under the **Placement** category and through the placement cell's student communications. Off-campus public-sector opportunities (such as the ISRO Technical Assistant recruitment with 84 posts) are also circulated to students.

### 13.8 Internship Opportunities

Internships are embedded in the programmes and supported by the placement cell and industry ties. The university runs an **Internship Policy** (published as INTERNSHIP-POLICY.pdf) and reports student internships in its AQAR filings. Internships include industry internships, live projects, and internship-to-placement conversions.

### 13.9 Salary Data (Highest / Average / Median Package)

The university's official public pages do not print highest/average/median package figures. The placement page describes the placement function and lists drives but presents no salary statistics.

**Chatbot rule:** For package questions, answer that the university does not publish a highest/average/median package figure on its public placement page; the university reports a **96% placement rate** as an institutional indicator; for verified salary data by branch and year, students and parents should request the official placement report from the Training & Placement Cell. Do not invent a package number.

### 13.10 Placement Percentage

Published institutional indicator: **96% placement rate** (homepage / About page institutional statistics).

### 13.11 Department-wise and Year-wise Placement

Placement outcomes are tracked department-wise by the placement cell and reported in the institutional AQAR filings, where "Number of outgoing students who got placement during the year" is disclosed per year. The annual placement reports are published as part of the accreditation document set. For branch-wise and year-wise verified figures, the chatbot should direct users to:

- the AQAR reports (published PDFs on the NAAC page), and
- the Training & Placement Cell's official placement report.

### 13.12 Job Roles

# PART 7 — Research & Ph.D.

*Checklist sections 15, 16 — official MRDU sources.*

## 15. RESEARCH

### 15.1 Research & Development Cell

The **R&D Cell** governs research at MRDU — Ph.D. administration, research policy, grants, publications, patents and research infrastructure. Official page: https://mrdu.edu.in/research.

Published R&D activity indicators:
- **650+ research scholars**
- **UGC-recognized R&D centres**
- **4-Star IIC rating** (Institution's Innovation Council)
- **80+ laboratories**, including research laboratories
- R&D host: workshops such as "Hands-On Workshop — IEEE Formatting Masterclass using Overleaf and Microsoft Word" and "Guest Lecture on Roadmap to High Impact PhD Research & Journal Publication"

### 15.2 Ph.D. Notifications

Published at https://mrdu.edu.in/research/phd. Documents include:
- **Ph.D. Programme Notification 2026-27** — official Ph.D. admission notification for AY 2026-27
- **Ph.D. Programme Notification 2025** — official notification for AY 2025-26
- **Ph.D. Admissions Notification 2026** (notifications feed, 22 May 2026)
- **Ph.D Entrance (MRDUCET) Examination Date Announced** (pinned notification)
- **MRDU Ph.D Regulations 2025-26** — listed under "Latest Regulations" with category *PhD Notifications*

### 15.3 Research Policies

Published at https://mrdu.edu.in/research/policies with sub-policies:

| Policy | Page |
|---|---|
| Research Policy | /research/policies/research-policy |
| Consultancy Policy | /research/policies/consultancy |
| Code of Ethics | /research/policies/code-of-ethics |
| Incentives Policy | /research/policies/incentives |
| Seed Grant Policy | /research/policies/seed-grant |

Additional research-related policy documents in the institutional library include the **IPR Policy** (IPR-Policy.pdf) and the **Internship Policy**.

### 15.4 Research Advisory Board

The research governance structure comprises the R&D Cell leadership, the Research Advisory Board, department-level research committees and the Ph.D. supervisors. Constitution of the research bodies is published with the research policy documents. (For current membership, the department R&D coordinator or the R&D Cell should be contacted.)

### 15.5 Ethics Committee

The institutional **Code of Ethics** (/research/policies/code-of-ethics) sets research ethics requirements including plagiarism, authorship, data integrity and human/animal subject norms. The **Institutional Ethics Committee (IEC) Guidelines** (IEC_Guidelines.pdf) are published among the university's compliance documents, alongside a UGC circular on the same subject.

### 15.6 EDC — Entrepreneurship Development Cell

The **Entrepreneurship Development Cell (EDC)** runs entrepreneurship programming. Published EDC activities:
- Inauguration of **Agri-Social Entrepreneurship Cell** and **Women Entrepreneurship Cell** (12 Sep 2026, Sports Block, MRDU)
- Talk on **CYBERPRENEURS: Inspiring Journeys of Innovation & Success** (21 Aug 2026, Sports Block, MRDU)
- **MSME Idea Hackathon 6.0**
- **IGNITE 2026 — Innovation & Entrepreneurship Summit** (Innovation & Entrepreneurship Summit showcasing startups, prototypes and industry collaborations)

### 15.7 BIC — Business Incubation Centre

Published at https://mrdu.edu.in/research/bic. The **Business Incubation Centre** incubates student and faculty startups within the university's innovation ecosystem, working alongside the Institution's Innovation Council (IIC, 4-Star) and the EDC. The university reports a "thriving startup ecosystem" as part of its research and innovation positioning.

### 15.8 Publications

Faculty and research-scholar publications are reported through:
- the **SSR Cycle III** report (comprehensive five-year research data)
- the **AQAR** reports 2020-21, 2021-22, 2022-23, 2023-24 (annual research, publication, patent and grant disclosures)
- the **NIRF submissions** (NIRF-2026-OVERALL, ENGINEERING, MANAGEMENT, INNOVATION, SDG) which include publications, citations and research funding
- the **NIRF Innovation Ranking** submission

### 15.9 Faculty Research Profiles

Department pages have a **Research** tab listing faculty research areas, and PhD Scholars and Projects sections. Individual faculty research profiles are maintained by departments. The R&D Cell coordinates the institutional faculty research profile registry (publications, projects, patents, awards).

### 15.10 Research Projects

Research projects are executed at department level and through the R&D Cell and the UGC-recognized R&D centres. Funding routes include:
- AICTE schemes (the institution reports **₹50 Lakh AICTE Margadarshan Scheme** support)
- Seed grants (internal Seed Grant Policy at /research/policies/seed-grant)
- Consultancy revenue (Consultancy Policy at /research/policies/consultancy)
- Industry-sponsored and collaborative projects (MoUs)

### 15.11 Research Supervisors

Ph.D. supervisors are drawn from among Professors and Ph.D.-qualified Associate Professors (the directory lists 56+ Professors and 72+ Associate Professors, with a large share holding Ph.D.) and are listed in the Ph.D. notification/supervisor list for each discipline. The scholar-supervisor allocation follows UGC Ph.D. Regulations 2022 norms.

### 15.12 Research Grants

Grant mechanisms published:
- **Seed Grant Policy** — internal seed funding for faculty research initiation
- **Incentives Policy** — incentives for publications, patents and funded projects
- **AICTE Margadarshan Scheme** — ₹50 Lakh institutional support
- External funding through DST/SERB, AICTE, UGC and industry collaborations

### 15.13 Patents

Patents are tracked by the R&D Cell and disclosed in AQAR/NIRF submissions. The **IPR Policy** governs invention disclosure, ownership, filing support and revenue sharing. For the current patent portfolio, the correct answer is to request the verified list from the R&D Cell rather than quoting a count from secondary sources.

### 15.14 Research Laboratories

Research laboratories exist within the 80+ laboratory estate, with R&D centres supported by tools including LabVIEW, IBM and HP DevOps platforms, Cadence, Ansys, Staad Pro and gas testing facilities.

### 15.15 Research Centres

The institution operates **UGC-recognized R&D centres** and a Centre of Excellence. Department-level research centres are listed on department Research tabs.

### 15.16 MoUs (Memoranda of Understanding)

MoU activity is reported through AQAR/SSR disclosures and news:
- **International collaboration MoU signed** — partnership to strengthen student exchange and joint research initiatives (news, 22 May 2026)
- Institution reports **1000+ collaborative activities per year** for research, faculty exchange, student exchange and internships
- Representatives of industry collaborators serve on institutional committees
- AWS Academy collaboration (CSE-AIML), Microsoft IoT initiative, and industry-sponsored design projects

### 15.17 Research Notifications

Published on https://mrdu.edu.in/research/phd and the Notifications page (categories: *PhD Notifications*, *Research*). Includes Ph.D. programme notifications, MRDUCET announcements, and Ph.D. regulations updates.

## 16. PH.D

### 16.1 Ph.D. Programmes

"Applications are invited for doctoral (Ph.D.) programmes for the academic year 2026–27 at Malla Reddy (MR) Deemed to be University. Formulated in accordance with **UGC Ph.D. Regulations 2022**."

Key published parameters:

| Parameter | Value |
|---|---|
| Cycle | 2026–27 |
| Disciplines | **10** |
| Regulatory framework | UGC Ph.D. Regulations 2022 |
| Selection framework | **70 : 30** |
| Entrance examination | **MRDUCET** + research interview |
| Direct entry | UGC-NET / CSIR-NET / GATE / JRF scholars |
| Modes | Full-time and Part-time |
| Coursework | Research Methodology & Ethics (comprehensive coursework) |

"The Ph.D. programme at Malla Reddy (MR) Deemed to be University is designed to develop independent researchers with strong analytical skills and advanced subject expertise. Adhering strictly to UGC Ph.D. Regulations 2022, scholars engage with expert supervisors, modern laboratory infrastructure, and industry-aligned research projects."

### 16.2 Ph.D. Disciplines (Offered 2026–27)

**Engineering & Technology**
1. Ph.D. Computer Science & Engineering
2. Ph.D. Electronics & Communication Engineering
3. Ph.D. Electrical & Electronics Engineering
4. Ph.D. Civil Engineering
5. Ph.D. Mechanical Engineering

**Sciences, Management and allied disciplines** — the Ph.D. admissions page lists the remaining disciplines under "Sciences, Manage…" (Sciences and Management streams), completing the **10 disciplines** advertised for the 2026–27 cycle. Disciplines in the sciences and management domain are confirmed in the Ph.D. notification PDF.

### 16.3 Ph.D. Eligibility

Per UGC Ph.D. Regulations 2022 and the MRDU notification:
- A **Master's degree** (or equivalent) in the relevant discipline from a recognised university with the minimum prescribed marks/grade.
- **Entrance route:** qualify in the **MRDUCET** entrance examination followed by a research interview.
- **Direct entry route:** candidates with **UGC-NET / CSIR-NET / GATE / JRF** qualifications are eligible for direct entry as per regulation.
- Programme-specific eligibility (minimum percentage, relaxation for reserved categories) is defined in the Ph.D. notification and Ph.D. Regulations 2025-26.

### 16.4 Ph.D. Admission

Application is made online via the Ph.D. admissions page (https://mrdu.edu.in/phdadmissions) with the "Apply Now" action for the 2026–27 cycle. The selection framework is **70:30** (entrance examination performance and interview/other components as defined in the regulations).

### 16.5 Ph.D. Entrance Examination (MRDUCET)

**MRDUCET** — the MRDU Common Entrance Test — is the university's doctoral entrance examination. The notification **"Ph.D Entrance (MRDUCET) Examination Date Announced"** is pinned to the top of the university homepage and the notifications feed, and is categorised as *General*. Candidates must check the Ph.D. admissions page for the announced examination date, syllabus, hall ticket and centre details.

### 16.6 Ph.D. Application Process

1. Read the **Ph.D. Programme Notification 2026-27** and the **MRDU Ph.D Regulations 2025-26**.
2. Confirm eligibility (master's degree; NET/GATE/JRF for direct entry).
3. Submit the online application through /phdadmissions ("Apply for Ph.D. Programme — Submit your application online for the 2026–27 Ph.D. admissions").
4. Appear for **MRDUCET** (if not claiming direct entry) and the research interview.
5. Receive the selection result; pay the prescribed Ph.D. fee to confirm admission.
6. Complete coursework (Research Methodology & Ethics) and begin research under an allotted supervisor.

### 16.7 Ph.D. Fee

The Ph.D. fee is published in the Ph.D. notification and the Ph.D. Regulations 2025-26 rather than on the general fees page (which publishes only UG ₹1,15,000/year and PG ₹60,000/year indicatively). The chatbot should quote the notification PDF and route fee queries to the research section through info@mrdu.edu.in.

### 16.8 Ph.D. Supervisors

Supervisors are drawn from Ph.D.-qualified Professors and Associate Professors. The supervisor list by discipline is published with the Ph.D. notification / R&D Cell communications. Under UGC Regulations 2022, a supervisor may guide a limited number of scholars as defined by the university.

### 16.9 Ph.D. Research Areas

Research areas align with the discipline and with the university's research strengths: AI/ML, data science, cyber security, IoT, cloud computing (CSE); VLSI, embedded systems, communication, signal processing (ECE); power systems, renewables, smart grid (EEE); structural engineering, sustainable construction, materials (Civil); thermal, manufacturing, robotics (Mechanical); plus sciences, mathematics, mining and management research.
