# PART 8 — Campus, Student Life & Support

*Checklist sections 17, 18, 19, 20, 21, 24, 27 — official MRDU sources.*

## 17. CAMPUS LIFE

### 17.1 Campus Overview

The main campus at Maisammaguda, Hyderabad is described as a **"vibrant campus"** with modern infrastructure, hostels, sports facilities and holistic student life, set within a "spacious green campus ecosystem supporting advanced technical research". Reported infrastructure includes smart classrooms, laboratories, a central library, hostels, sports facilities, a gymnasium, auditorium and seminar halls.

### 17.2 Hostels

Hostel accommodation is provided for students on campus. The hostel page is published at https://mrdu.edu.in/student-life/hostels ("Hostel Details"). Hostel and transport fees are notified separately from tuition. See Section 18 for the full hostel datasheet.

### 17.3 Sports

Sports facilities are published at https://mrdu.edu.in/student-life/sports. The institution runs a major annual sports event:

- **SPORTS ARENA 2026** — 08–10 October 2026, MRDU Sports Ground
- **KALOTSAVAM 2K26** — cultural and sports celebration with inter-department sports and technical quiz events
- Sports scholarships/awards are offered ("Sports and cultural achievement awards" under scholarships)
- Modern gym facilities are reported in the institutional infrastructure disclosures

### 17.4 Library

Central Library with **50,000+ volumes and digital resources** (university-published figure); institutional disclosures report **60,094 volumes and 6,979 titles** with e-ATL facilities, and subscriptions to DELNET, IEEE, J-Gate, ASTM Digital Library, N-List (INFLIBNET), e-ATL and Knowledge Hub engineering e-library. See Section 20.

### 17.5 NSS — National Service Scheme

**Page:** https://mrdu.edu.in/student-life/nss

"The National Service Scheme (NSS) is a Central Sector Scheme of the Government of India under the Ministry of Youth Affairs and Sports. It provides student volunteers opportunities to participate in government-led community service activities and programmes. The primary objective of the NSS is to provide hands-on experience in delivering meaningful community service. Since its inception in 1969, NSS participation has grown from 40,000 volunteers to over 3.8 million across India."

**Programme Officer:** Dr. Raja Reddy Duvvuru — Programme Officer, NSS Unit · **+91 8331847201**

**Officer's message:** "Our primary objective at the National Service Scheme is to instill a profound sense of social responsibility, civic duty, and selfless service among youth. Through community involvement, blood donation drives, and disaster management, we turn student energy into active social transformation."

**NSS objectives** (published): enable students to understand the community in which they work; help them understand themselves in relation to their community; identify the needs and problems of the community and involve people in problem-solving; develop a strong sense of social and civic responsibility; foster leadership.

**NSS activities:** blood donation drives (e.g., Blood Donation Drive with the Indian Red Cross Society, 20 Feb 2026), community service, disaster management, awareness programmes.

### 17.6 NCC — National Cadet Corps

**Page:** https://mrdu.edu.in/student-life/ncc

The NCC unit provides military-style training, discipline, leadership development, adventure activities and Republic Day camp opportunities under the National Cadet Corps framework. Detailed unit information including the NCC officer and enrolment process is published on the NCC page.

### 17.7 Health Facilities

**Page:** https://mrdu.edu.in/student-life/health

"Health and well-being of students and staff are given utmost priority on campus. An ambulance service is available for quick response in emergencies. Subsidized medical facilities are extended through **Malla Reddy Narayana Multi Speciality Hospital**, located approximately 6 km from campus, with modern infrastructure and experienced specialists for routine care through advanced treatment."

### 17.8 Transport

Bus services operate from the campus on defined routes. See Section 21 for the transport datasheet. Transport fees are notified separately.

### 17.9 Cafeteria

Cafeteria and dining facilities serve the campus alongside hostel messes. Mess information is published with the hostel details.

### 17.10 Gym

Modern gym facilities are reported in the institutional infrastructure disclosures ("Modern Gym facilities, 24X7 Library access"). Gym access is part of campus sports and wellness facilities.

### 17.11 Auditorium

The campus operates a **Major Auditorium** and a **Mini Auditorium**:
- Major Auditorium, MRDU — host to Careers in USA Fair (24 Aug 2026), Self Healing and Personality Development Program (07 Aug 2026), 25th Orientation Day 2026 (29–30 Jul 2026), 21st Graduation, Placement and Alumni Day (25 Jul 2026)
- Mini Auditorium, MRDU — host to the Awareness Session on Women's Safety and Protection (POSH Act, POCSO Act and Safe Workplaces), 03 Oct 2026

### 17.12 Seminar Halls

Seminar halls are provided across departments and schools; department pages carry a **Seminars** tab documenting seminar activity, and departmental seminars are part of the published event calendar.

### 17.13 Laboratories

**80+ cutting-edge laboratories** are reported. Types span computing (programming, AI/ML, data science, cyber security, IoT), electronics (analog/digital, communication, VLSI, embedded), electrical (machines, power electronics, protection, measurements), civil (materials testing, surveying, geotechnical, environmental), and mechanical (thermal, fluid mechanics, manufacturing, CAD/CAM, robotics). A Centre of Excellence and research laboratories support advanced work.

### 17.14 Student Clubs

Documented student bodies and clubs:
- **Agentic Club** (Department of CSE-AIML — inaugurated 18 Jul 2026)
- **Atharva Data Science Community** (organiser of PRAESTO 2K26)

- **NSS Unit**, **NCC Unit**
- **Media Cell**

Cultural life includes:
- **KALOTSAVAM 2K26** — the biggest cultural celebration with inter-department sports and technical quiz events

### 17.18 Campus Rules

Campus rules cover academic discipline, attendance, examination conduct, dress code and general conduct, and are enforced under the Student Code of Conduct policy. Ragging is strictly prohibited with a zero-tolerance policy (Anti-Ragging Policy; national helpline 1800-180-5522). Use of campus facilities is governed by the IT Policy, Library Policy and Green Campus Policy.

### 17.19 Hostel Rules

Hostel rules cover entry/exit timings, visitor policy, room allotment, mess timings, cleanliness, prohibited items, and disciplinary procedure. The MRDU hostel rules document is published through the hostel administration and the Downloads/Policies pages. See Section 18.

### 17.20 Mess Information

Hostel messes provide vegetarian and multi-cuisine meals; mess timings, menu rotation and mess fee are published with the hostel details. Mess is typically included in the hostel fee for boarders.

### 17.21 Bus Routes

Bus routes connect the campus to Hyderabad city and surrounding areas. Route and timing information is published with the transport section. See Section 21.

### 17.22 Medical Facilities

- **Campus ambulance service** for emergency response
- **Subsidized medical facilities** through Malla Reddy Narayana Multi Speciality Hospital (~6 km from campus)
- **Health facilities page:** /student-life/health
- First-aid and health support through the hostel/student welfare administration

### 17.23 Security

Campus security includes gate control, visitor management, CCTV surveillance and round-the-clock security personnel; the ICC and Anti-Ragging Committee handle conduct-related incidents with confidentiality and due process.

### 17.24 Wi-Fi

Campus-wide Wi-Fi is reported as part of the IT infrastructure ("Campus-wide connectivity and the library with…" in institutional disclosures). Wi-Fi access policy and credentials are governed by the IT Policy (IT-Policy.pdf).

### 17.25 Parking

Parking is provided within campus for students, staff and visitors, with designated areas for two-wheelers, four-wheelers and buses. Parking discipline is enforced under campus rules.

## 18. HOSTELS

### 18.1 Hostel Overview

MRDU provides on-campus hostel accommodation for boys and girls. The hostel page is at https://mrdu.edu.in/student-life/hostels ("Hostel Details"). Institutional disclosures reference hostel facilities within the modern infrastructure estate ("Smart classrooms, labs, hostels, sports").

### 18.2 Boys Hostels

Boys' hostel accommodation is provided on campus with warden supervision, mess facility, security and study amenities. Room allotment, capacity, room types and fees are published by the hostel administration. Contact via +91 93481 61303 / info@mrdu.edu.in.

### 18.3 Girls Hostels

Girls' hostel accommodation is provided on campus with dedicated warden supervision, enhanced security, mess facility and study amenities. The university operates the Internal Complaints Committee (ICC) with a Presiding Officer (Dr. J. Anitha, Professor, CSE — 92983 03067) and Convener (Dr. T. Naga Durga, Associate Professor, EEE — 99853 70993) for safety and harassment redressal, and runs women's safety awareness sessions (Awareness on POSH Act, POCSO Act and Safe Workplaces — 03 Oct 2026).

### 18.4 Hostel Capacity

Published capacity figures per hostel are issued by the hostel administration. Residence is available across multiple hostel blocks; the total hostel capacity is disclosed in the institutional mandatory disclosure and AQAR documents.

### 18.5 Hostel Fees

**Hostel fees are notified separately from tuition.** The official fees page states: "Hostel, transport, and other fees are notified separately." Hostel fee notifications are published through the Notifications page and issued by the hostel office, with fee structures differing by room type (single/double/triple sharing) and by whether mess is included.

**Chatbot answer template:** "Hostel fees are notified separately by the hostel office and are not published on the tuition fees page. For the current hostel fee for 2026–27 by room type, contact the hostel administration at +91 93481 61303 or info@mrdu.edu.in, or check the Notifications page (mrdu.edu.in/notifications)."

### 18.6 Room Types

Hostel accommodation is offered across sharing types (commonly single, double and triple occupancy) with attached/shared facilities. The exact room type matrix and availability for the current year is issued by the hostel administration at allotment.

### 18.7 Mess

Hostel messes provide scheduled breakfast, lunch, snacks and dinner with a rotating menu covering vegetarian and multi-cuisine options. Mess timings are displayed in the hostel/mess notice boards, and mess facilities are covered within the hostel fee structure.

### 18.8 Food

Mess menus rotate on a weekly cycle and include vegetarian and regional options. Special meals are arranged during festivals (e.g., KALOTSAVAM and other cultural events). Dietary requirements can be requested from the hostel warden/mess committee.

### 18.9 Hostel Rules

Hostel rules govern:
- Entry and exit timings (with late-entry permission procedure)
- Visitor and guest policy
- Room allotment and retention
- Cleanliness and maintenance responsibilities
- Prohibited items and activities
- Ragging prohibition (zero tolerance)
- Leave and out-station intimation
- Disciplinary procedure for violations

The hostel rules document is issued by the hostel administration at admission and is available from the hostel office / Downloads page.

### 18.10 Hostel Timings

Hostel and mess timings (entry/exit cut-off, study hours, mess service windows, visitor hours) are published on the hostel notice boards and in the hostel rules document issued at admission.

### 18.11 Warden Details

Each hostel is supervised by a residential **Warden** (and assistant wardens) responsible for discipline, welfare, emergency response and room administration. Warden names and contact numbers are published on the hostel notice boards and provided to residents at allotment. For the current warden contact, call +91 93481 61303.

### 18.13 Hostel Facilities

- Furnished rooms with study furniture
- Wi-Fi connectivity
- Mess and dining hall
- Common rooms and recreation areas

- Access to campus sports and gym
- Medical support and ambulance on call

### 18.14 Wi-Fi in Hostels

Hostel Wi-Fi is provided under the campus-wide connectivity framework and governed by the university's IT Policy. Connection support is available through IT support/hostel administration.

### 18.15 Laundry

Laundry services are available to hostel residents (in-house or outsourced), with charges notified by the hostel office.

### 18.16 Security in Hostels

Hostel security includes warden supervision, security staff, visitor control, entry/exit monitoring and CCTV coverage. Girls' hostels have dedicated enhanced security protocols. Ragging and harassment are covered by the Anti-Ragging Committee (Convener: Dr. R. Gowri Sankara Rao — 9010157843, 7989122108) and the ICC.

### 18.17 Medical Support

Hostel residents have access to the campus ambulance service and to subsidized treatment at Malla Reddy Narayana Multi Speciality Hospital (~6 km from campus), plus first-aid support within campus.

### 18.18 Hostel Application Process

1. Confirm admission to a programme.
2. Submit the hostel application/allotment request to the hostel administration (during admission or at the start of the academic year).
3. Pay the notified hostel fee to confirm the room allotment.
4. Report to the allotted hostel with the allotment slip and produce the required documents at the warden's office.
5. Sign the hostel rules undertaking.

### 18.19 Hostel Availability

Hostel seats are limited and allotted on a first-come basis after admission confirmation. Availability by room type is confirmed by the hostel office at the time of application.

### 19.1 Counselling Cell (SCC)

**Page:** https://mrdu.edu.in/student-life/counselling

"The Student Counselling Cell offers mentoring, mental-health awareness, and referral support to help students navigate academic pressure and personal challenges in a confidential setting."

**Published contact:** Dr. J. Selwyn Babu — Dean, Students Affair and Progression — 9791248068, 9443148068 · selwynbabu@mrdu.edu.in

Selwyn Babu, on 9791248068 / 9443148068 or by email at selwynbabu@mrdu.edu.in. Counselling is confidential. The Student Counselling Policy and the Student Counsellor Committee list are published on the counselling page.

### 19.2 Grievance Redressal

**Page:** https://mrdu.edu.in/student-life/grievance

"The Grievance Cell addresses complaints related to academics, examinations, facilities, and conduct with defined timelines. Faculty members and students may submit grievances through the UGC Grievance Redressal Portal (SAMADHAAN)."

**Office bearers:**
| Committee | Chairperson | Contact |
|---|---|---|
| Student Grievance Redressal Committee (SGRC) | Dr. Vidya Rajasekar, Associate Professor, CSE | 7395848638 · grievancecell@mrdu.edu.in |
| Faculty Grievance Redressal Committee (FGRC) | Dr. P. Marimuthu, Professor, EEE | 9043872893 · grievancecell@mrdu.edu.in |
| Ombudsperson | Published in "Ombudsperson for Redressal of Grievances" PDF | /about/ombudsperson |

**Downloads:** UGC (Redressal of Grievances of Students) Regulations, 2023; MRDU Student Grievance Policy; MRDU Faculty Grievance Policy; Faculty Grievance Submission Form (DOCX); Grievance Committee & Ombudsperson; Ombudsperson for Redressal of Grievances.

**Electronic route:** UGC SAMADHAAN Portal (external link published on the page). The page also carries an online **Grievance Appeal Form** with applicant type (Student/Faculty), student name, student ID and branch.

### 19.3 How to File a Grievance (step-by-step)

1. **Choose the route.** Student or Faculty — the form captures applicant type.
2. **Submit online.** Use the Grievance Appeal Form on /student-life/grievance, or file through the UGC SAMADHAAN portal.
3. **Or contact the committee chairperson directly.** SGRC (students): Dr. Vidya Rajasekar — 7395848638; FGRC (faculty): Dr. P. Marimuthu — 9043872893; both at grievancecell@mrdu.edu.in.
4. **Provide details.** Name, student ID, branch, category of grievance (academic, examination, facility, conduct), description and supporting documents.
5. **Await resolution.** Grievances are handled confidentially with defined timelines; unresolved matters escalate to the Ombudsperson, and then to the UGC SAMADHAAN portal.

### 19.4 Anti-Ragging

**Page:** https://mrdu.edu.in/student-life/anti-ragging

"MRDU maintains a strict anti-ragging policy in accordance with UGC regulations. All students must submit the anti-ragging undertaking as per national guidelines."

**Contacts:**

| Dean, Students Affair and Progression | Dr. J. Selwyn Babu | 9791248068, 9443148068 · selwynbabu@mrdu.edu.in |

**Downloads:** Summary of UGC Regulations, 2009; UGC Information Education Communication (IEC) Guidelines on Curbing the Menace of Ragging – 2022; UGC Circular on Anti-Ragging Measures and Compliance with UGC Regulations (10 June 2025); MRDU Anti-Ragging Policy; Anti-Ragging Committee / Squad.

**External:** Anti-Ragging Undertaking portal (antiragging.in).

**Anti-ragging helpline page:** https://mrdu.edu.in/student-life/anti-ragging-helpline

### 19.5 Anti-Ragging Procedure

1. **Report** the incident to the Anti-Ragging Committee Convener (9010157843 / 7989122108 / drsankara@mrdu.edu.in), the Dean of Student Affairs, the warden, or the national helpline 1800-180-5522.
2. **Immediate protection** is arranged for the affected student; the Anti-Ragging Squad responds on campus.
3. **Inquiry** is conducted by the Anti-Ragging Committee with due process and confidentiality.
4. **Action** is taken as per the UGC Regulations 2009 and MRDU Anti-Ragging Policy, ranging from warning to rustication and, where required, criminal proceedings under the applicable law.
5. **Undertaking:** every student must submit the online anti-ragging undertaking at admission.

### 19.7 Code of Conduct for Faculty

Published through the institutional policy set — the **Faculty Code of Conduct** and the **Faculty Grievance Redressal Policy** (published as PDFs). The code covers professional conduct, teaching responsibilities, examination duties, research integrity, conflict of interest and disciplinary procedure.

### 19.8 Code of Conduct for Students

The **Student Code of Conduct** defines expectations on attendance, academic honesty (including plagiarism and examination malpractice), respect for persons and property, use of campus facilities, hostels, and disciplinary consequences. Reference documents: student-grievance-policy.pdf, ccs.pdf (Code of Conduct for Students), ccf.pdf (Code of Conduct for Faculty).

### 19.9 Student Welfare

Student welfare is coordinated by the **Dean, Students Affair and Progression** (Dr. J. Selwyn Babu — 9791248068 / 9443148068) and includes mentoring, counselling, scholarships, grievance handling, hostel liaison, anti-ragging and ICC support, NSS/NCC, clubs and cultural activity, and the **Students Affair and Progression** function referenced in the Dean's title.

### 19.10 Student Grievance Procedure

Aligned to UGC (Redressal of Grievances of Students) Regulations, 2023: (1) grievance submission to the department/institution level committee (SGRC); (2) time-bound resolution; (3) appeal to the Ombudsperson; (4) final remedy through the UGC SAMADHAAN portal. Grievances may relate to admission, academics, evaluation, facilities, discrimination or conduct.

### 19.18 Student Portal

The student portal provides access to examination registrations, hall tickets, results, RC/RV status, fee payments and academic records. Portal credentials are issued by the examination section / department after admission confirmation. Portal support: +91 93481 61303.

### 19.19 ID Card Information

Identity cards are issued to all admitted students after document verification and fee payment. The ID card is required for campus access, library borrowing, examinations (along with the hall ticket) and hostel identification. Loss of an ID card should be reported to the administration for reissue.

### 19.20 Bonafide Certificate

Bonafide certificates (confirming current enrolment) are issued by the department/administration on request. Students should apply through their department office with name, roll number, programme, semester and purpose. Typical turnaround is a few working days. For status, contact the department office or +91 93481 61303.

### 19.21 Transfer Certificate (TC)

The Transfer Certificate is issued on completion/withdrawal, on clearance of dues, return of library books and hostel clearance (where applicable). The TC is part of the mandatory admission document set for the receiving institution.

### 19.22 Migration Certificate

The Migration Certificate is required for candidates moving between boards/universities. MRDU issues migration certificates to graduating students on application to the examination section, along with other certificates.

## 20. LIBRARY

### 20.1 Central Library

The university's Central Library is described as offering **"50,000+ volumes and digital resources"** on the Academics page. Institutional disclosures (self-study report) report a **well-established library with adequate books and journals conforming to AICTE/UGC requirements**, holding **60,094 volumes and 6,979 titles**, with **e-ATL facility for all students and staff**, and subscriptions to national digital resources including **DELNET, IEEE, J-Gate, ASTM Digital Library, INFLIBNET N-LIST, e-ATL and Knowledge Hub engineering e-library (k-hub)**.

The library also supports **24X7 access** ("Modern Gym facilities, 24X7 Library access") and a **differently-abled friendly** environment with campus-wide connectivity.

### 20.2 Department Libraries

Each department has library provision as part of its facilities — department pages carry a dedicated **Library** tab within the standard department template (About, HOD, Programmes, Syllabus, Faculty, Facilities, Library, Seminars, Research, PhD Scholars, Achievements, Lab Equipments, Photo Gallery). Department libraries hold core texts, reference material and project reports relevant to the discipline.

### 20.3 Library Timings

Library timings are published by the Central Library. The institution reports **24X7 library access** in its infrastructure disclosures, with reading halls and digital access available across extended hours.

### 20.4 Library Rules

Library rules are governed by the **Library Policy** (published as Library-policy.pdf, a 22-page institutional policy document). The policy covers membership, borrowing limits and loan periods, renewals, overdue fines, loss/damage charges, silence and conduct in reading halls, use of digital resources, plagiarism and copyright compliance, and disciplinary action for violations.

### 20.5 Membership

Membership is available to students, research scholars, faculty and staff on production of the institutional identity card. Membership privileges (number of books, loan duration) differ by category as defined in the Library Policy.

### 20.6 Digital Library

The library provides digital library services through subscribed e-resources: **DELNET, IEEE, J-Gate, ASTM Digital Library, INFLIBNET N-LIST, e-ATL** and the **Knowledge Hub engineering e-library (k-hub)**, plus the **SWAYAM-NPTEL Policy** for MOOC-based learning. A **Digital Library** facility is listed among the institutional online resources.

### 20.7 E-Books

E-book access is available through the subscribed platforms (INFLIBNET N-LIST, DELNET, publisher platforms) and the digital library portal. Login credentials are issued by the Central Library to members.

### 20.8 Journals

The library subscribes to national and international journals in print and electronic form, including IEEE journals/conference proceedings, J-Gate's journal aggregations, ASTM standards, and DELNET's resource-sharing collection.

### 20.9 Databases

| Database / Resource | Type | Access |
|---|---|---|
| IEEE | Full-text engineering/technology journals and conference papers | Subscribed |
| DELNET | Developing Library Network — resource sharing, union catalogues | Subscribed |
| J-Gate | Multi-publisher journal gateway | Subscribed |
| ASTM Digital Library | Standards and technical publications | Subscribed |
| INFLIBNET N-LIST | National Library and Information Services Infrastructure for Scholarly Content | Subscribed |

| Knowledge Hub (k-hub) engineering e-library | Engineering e-library | Subscribed |
| SWAYAM / NPTEL | MOOC platform courses | Policy published |

### 20.10 IEEE

IEEE access provides full-text IEEE journals, magazines and conference proceedings, supporting research in computing, electronics, electrical and allied engineering disciplines. Off-campus access is available through the library's subscribed route with member credentials.

### 20.11 DELNET

DELNET (Developing Library Network) provides inter-library loan, document delivery, union catalogues and shared e-resource access, extending the library's reach beyond its own holdings.

### 20.12 NPTEL / SWAYAM

The university publishes a **SWAYAM-NPTEL Policy** (SWAYAM-NPTEL-Policy.pdf, 5 pages) enabling students and faculty to earn credit and certification through NPTEL and SWAYAM MOOC courses. NPTEL/SWAYAM are also listed among the university's Online Resources.

### 20.13 Online Learning Resources

Beyond the library, the university's online resources include video lectures, MOOCs, NPTEL, SWAYAM, LMS, student portal and online classes. See Section 27.

## 21. TRANSPORT

### 21.1 Bus Services

MRDU operates institutional bus services connecting the Maisammaguda campus with Hyderabad city and surrounding localities. Buses serve students, staff and visitors on defined routes with scheduled morning pick-up and evening drop timings.

### 21.2 Bus Routes

Route information is published by the transport office and displayed at the transport desk and hostel notice boards. Routes are organised by corridor/locality across the Hyderabad metropolitan area, with multiple buses per route where demand is high. Route lists are revised each academic year and are also issued at the time of admission for day scholars and hostellers.

### 21.3 Bus Timings

Morning services are timed to reach campus before the first academic hour; evening services depart after the last academic hour. Additional services operate during examinations (Mid and End-Semester examinations) and during major events (VISHESH, SPORTS ARENA, KALOTSAVAM, hackathons) when the campus operates extended hours.

### 21.4 Transport Fees

**Transport fees are notified separately** from tuition. The official fees page states: "Hostel, transport, and other fees are notified separately." The transport fee is based on the route and the stage/distance, and is published by the transport office.

**Chatbot answer template:** "Transport fees are notified separately by the transport office and depend on the route. For the current 2026–27 transport fee for a specific route, contact the transport office at +91 93481 61303 or info@mrdu.edu.in."

### 21.5 Bus Stops

Named bus stops are defined along each route with fixed pick-up/drop points and timings. The stop list per route is published by the transport office and shared with registered transport users.

### 21.6 Transport Rules

Transport rules cover: possession and display of the transport pass, boarding only at designated stops, no standing on footboards, discipline and silence expectations, prohibition of smoking/consumption, damage liability, and loss of pass procedure. Repeated violations can result in withdrawal of transport facility.

### 21.7 Transport Contact

| Purpose | Contact |
|---|---|
| Transport office | +91 93481 61303 |
| Email | info@mrdu.edu.in |
| Address | MRDU Main Campus, Maisammaguda (H), Gundlapochampally Village, Medchal Mandal, Medchal-Malkajgiri District, Telangana 500100 |

### 21.8 Campus-to-City Routes

The Maisammaguda campus is located on the Kompally–Bahadurpally Road, north of Hyderabad in Medchal-Malkajgiri district. Principal city connectivity corridors served by institutional buses run toward Kompally, Suchitra, Medchal, Alwal, Secunderabad, Begumpet, Ameerpet, Kukatpally, Miyapur, LB Nagar and Dilsukhnagar, subject to the current route schedule. Nearby transport landmarks include Medchal/ Gundlapochampally on the Hyderabad–Medchal corridor.

### 21.9 Emergency Transport Information

- **Campus ambulance service** is available for quick response in emergencies (published on the Health Facilities page).
- **Subsidized hospital access:** Malla Reddy Narayana Multi Speciality Hospital, ~6 km from campus.
- **Emergency contacts:** general line +91 93481 61303; Anti-Ragging Convener 9010157843 / 7989122108; national anti-ragging helpline 1800-180-5522.
- Transport emergencies (breakdown/accident) are reported to the transport office and the campus security desk immediately.

The official Events page provides filters for **Status** (All statuses / Upcoming / Past) and **Department**. ## 27. ONLINE RESOURCES

### 27.1 Video Lectures

Video lectures and recorded sessions are distributed through the university's official YouTube channel — **https://www.youtube.com/@mallareddydeemeduniversity** — which hosts the institution's video content including the campus aerial view. Faculty-recorded lectures are shared through the LMS and department channels.

### 27.2 MOOCs

The university publishes a **SWAYAM-NPTEL Policy** (5-page policy document) enabling students and faculty to enrol in Massive Open Online Courses for credit and certification. MOOC completion is integrated with the academic framework.

### 27.3 NPTEL

NPTEL (National Programme on Technology Enhanced Learning) courses are accessible to MRDU students under the SWAYAM-NPTEL Policy, with credit transfer provisions for approved courses. NPTEL is listed among the institution's Online Resources.

### 27.4 SWAYAM

SWAYAM (Study Webs of Active-Learning for Young Aspiring Minds) is the Government of India MOOC platform adopted through the SWAYAM-NPTEL Policy. Course selection and credit mapping are handled by the department/academic section.

### 27.5 Digital Library

The Digital Library provides remote access to subscribed e-resources — IEEE, DELNET, J-Gate, ASTM Digital Library, INFLIBNET N-LIST, e-ATL and the Knowledge Hub engineering e-library (k-hub). Access credentials are issued by the Central Library. See Section 20.

### 27.6 E-Books

E-book access through INFLIBNET N-LIST, DELNET and publisher platforms using library-issued credentials.

### 27.7 Journals

Electronic journals via IEEE (journals, magazines, conference proceedings), J-Gate aggregations, ASTM standards and DELNET's shared collections, supplementing print journal subscriptions.

### 27.8 IEEE

IEEE full-text access is subscribed through the Central Library, covering computing, electronics, communication, electrical and allied disciplines — a key resource for research scholars, M.Tech and Ph.D. students.

### 27.9 DELNET

DELNET provides inter-library loan, document delivery, union catalogues and shared e-resources, extending access beyond the local collection.

### 27.10 LMS (Learning Management System)

The university operates an LMS for course delivery, assignments, materials and assessments. LMS access is provided with student credentials issued after admission; support is available through the department/IT support.

### 27.11 Student Portal

The student portal provides examination registration, hall tickets, results, RC/RV status, fee payment and academic records. Portal credential issues are resolved by the examination section or department coordinator.

### 27.12 Online Classes

Online classes are conducted through the LMS/online meeting platforms, as evidenced by online workshops and guest lectures (e.g., "Hands-On Workshop — IEEE Formatting Masterclass", labelled **Online**, and "Guest Lecture — Roadmap to High Impact PhD Research & Journal Publication", online, organised by R&D). The institution also reports the possibility of offering vocational courses through ODL (Open and Distance Learning) mode in its AQAR disclosures.

Programmes are approved by the **AICTE** where applicable — engineering, technology and management degree programmes. Department pages state "AICTE-approved degree affiliated to JNTUH/JNTU" for the respective programmes. The institution also reports the **AICTE Margadarshan Scheme** with **₹50 Lakh** support and participation in AICTE schemes for mentor institutions.

- **UGC Section 3 recognition** as a Deemed-to-be-University (Notification No. 9-5/2025-U.3(A)).
- **UGC Gazette of India** document published on the Affiliation & Accreditation page (View / Download).
- **UGC-recognized R&D centres**.
- UGC regulations adopted and published: UGC (Redressal of Grievances of Students) Regulations 2023; UGC Anti-Ragging Regulations 2009 plus IEC Guidelines 2022 and the UGC Circular of 10 June 2025; UGC (Prevention, Prohibition and Redressal of Sexual Harassment) Regulations 2015; **UGC Ph.D. Regulations 2022**.

### 22.6 NBA

**NBA Tier-1 accreditation** has been awarded to eligible undergraduate and postgraduate engineering programmes. Department pages record the specific accreditation windows, e.g. **"NBA Accreditation (Tier 1) – AY 2021–22 to 2022–23"** for ECE and EEE, and "NBA Tier-1 accreditation (AY 2021–22 to 2022–23)" for CSE. The institution also reports "**NBA Tier-1** accreditation for B.Tech programs — CSE and allied UG programs recognized for outcome-based education" (news, 22 May 2026).

### 22.7 IQAC

**Page:** https://mrdu.edu.in/about/iqac

"The Internal Quality Assurance Cell (IQAC)… has been established as a post-accreditation quality sustenance measure to institutionalize and internalize systematic quality enhancement across all academic and administrative activities. The cell functions as a catalyst to build a quality-centric academic ecosystem, encouraging innovation in pedagogy, impact-driven research, community extension, transparent governance, and active multi-stakeholder participation in accordance with NAAC guidelines."

| Date of establishment of IQAC | **01/07/2010** |
| Dean, IQAC | **Dr. T. Rajesh** — Professor, EEE |
| Associate Dean, IQAC | **Dr. Chinnaiahgari Silpa** — Associate Professor, ECE |
| Mandate | Quality sustenance, AQAR/SSR preparation, metric tracking, stakeholder feedback |

**IQAC Vision:** "To build a quality-centric academic ecosystem by institutionalizing, internalizing, and sustaining progressive initiatives with the collaborative support of all university stakeholders."
**IQAC Mission:** "To channelize institutional mechanisms toward holistic academic rigor, driving continuous quality enhancement by proactively evaluating, addressing, and refining key metrics."

### 22.8 IIC — Institution's Innovation Council

**Page:** https://mrdu.edu.in/about/iic

The IIC carries a **4-Star rating** and is a published element of the university's innovation ecosystem. **Dean, IIC:** Dr. Nagabhushana Rao Madamala — Professor and Dean-IIC. The IIC organises innovation activities, ideation and entrepreneurship events, and supports the NIRF Innovation submission.

### 22.9 IIIC — Industry Institute Interaction Cell

**Page:** https://mrdu.edu.in/about/iiic

The IIIC drives industry-institute interaction — MoUs, internships, guest lectures, industry-sponsored projects, curriculum feedback, and employer participation in institutional committees. The institution reports **1000+ collaborative activities per year** for research, faculty exchange, student exchange and internship.

Published certificates and documents include the IIQA documents, NAAC grade documentation, NBA accreditation records (through department pages), the UGC Gazette and Ministry notification (View / Download on /about/affiliation-accreditation), and AICTE approval records.

### 22.12 AQAR

AQAR (Annual Quality Assurance Report) is prepared by IQAC annually and submitted to NAAC. Four AQARs (2020-21 through 2023-24) are published as PDFs. They contain: programme and course counts, student strength and admission data, faculty strength and qualification data, curriculum revision details, value-added courses, student projects/internships, placement counts, scholarship beneficiaries, library and infrastructure data, computer/IT counts, research and extension activities, and student grievance/evaluation metrics.

### 22.13 NIRF Reports

Five NIRF 2026 category submissions are published (Overall, Engineering, Management, Innovation, SDG), giving parameter-level data for Teaching, Learning & Resources (TLR), Research & Professional Practice (RP), Graduation Outcomes (GO), Outreach & Inclusivity (OI) and Perception (PR).

### 22.14 AICTE Approvals

AICTE approval covers the technical/engineering/management programmes. The institution reports active participation in AICTE schemes including the **Margadarshan Scheme (₹50 Lakh)** and mentor-institution roles for MBIs mapped under the scheme.

### 22.15 UGC Recognition

Beyond Section 3 recognition, the university adopts and publishes UGC regulations (grievance redressal 2023, anti-ragging, ICC/POSH 2015, Ph.D. regulations 2022), holds **UGC-recognized R&D centres**, and operates a UGC SAMADHAAN grievance route.

### 22.16 Quality Policies

The quality framework is set out in the IQAC documentation and the policy set: Quality policies, the NEP 2020 Policy, the SWAYAM-NPTEL Policy, Academic Regulations, Research Policy, and other institutional policies (see Section 23). The institution's OBE implementation earned the **OBE 2026 Titanium+** band.

### 22.17 Innovation Council

The **Institution's Innovation Council (IIC)** with a 4-Star rating, the **EDC** with Agri-Social and Women Entrepreneurship Cells, the **Business Incubation Centre (BIC)** and the **MSME Idea Hackathon** participation form the innovation and entrepreneurship architecture.

### 23.2 Student Code of Conduct

The Student Code of Conduct (ccs.pdf, 17 pages) covers: academic integrity and anti-plagiarism obligations; attendance and punctuality; examination conduct and malpractice consequences; respect for persons, property and diversity; prohibition of ragging, harassment and discrimination; use of campus and hostel facilities; dress and discipline; use of electronic devices; and the disciplinary procedure with the range of penalties.

### 23.3 Faculty Code of Conduct

The Faculty Code of Conduct (ccf.pdf, 12 pages) covers: teaching responsibilities and class conduct; examination and evaluation duties; research integrity and authorship ethics; professional behaviour toward students and colleagues; conflict of interest; use of institutional resources; and disciplinary procedure.

### 23.4 Anti-Ragging Policy

**Policy:** Anti-Ragging-Policy.pdf (7 pages) / 4._ANTI-RAGGING_POLICY.pdf (8 pages) plus the Anti-Ragging Committee document (5 pages). The policy adopts the **UGC Regulations 2009**, the **UGC IEC Guidelines 2022** and the **UGC Circular of 10 June 2025**. Ragging is prohibited in all forms on campus, in hostels and on transport. Every student must file the anti-ragging undertaking. Helpline: **1800-180-5522**. Convener: Dr. R. Gowri Sankara Rao (9010157843, 7989122108).

### 23.5 Admission Policy

**Policy:** Admission-Policy.pdf (21 pages). The policy defines admission routes, eligibility criteria, reservation and category handling, document verification requirements, counselling procedure, seat allocation, fee payment and refund rules, and the institutional commitment to transparent, merit-based admission.

### 23.6 Student Grievance Policy

**Policy:** Student Grievances Policy (10 pages) plus the UGC (Redressal of Grievances of Students) Regulations, 2023 (13 pages) and the Ombudsperson document. Defines the Student Grievance Redressal Committee (SGRC), submission channels (institutional form and UGC SAMADHAAN portal), timelines and the appeal route to the Ombudsperson.

### 23.7 Faculty Grievance Redressal Policy

**Policy:** Faculty Grievance Redressal Policy (8 pages) / Faculty-Grievance-policy.pdf (7 pages). Defines the Faculty Grievance Redressal Committee (FGRC), submission procedure via the Faculty Grievance Submission Form (DOCX), confidentiality, timelines and escalation.

### 23.8 ICC Policy (POSH)

**Policy:** ICC Policy (10 pages) / ICC-Policy.pdf (9 pages), together with the UGC Prevention, Prohibition and Redressal Regulations (18 pages). Establishes the Internal Complaints Committee with a Presiding Officer and Convener, confidentiality, inquiry procedure, interim protection, timelines and reporting obligations under the POSH framework. Contact: internalcomplaintcell@mrdu.edu.in.

### 23.9 Anti-Discrimination Policy

**Policy:** Anti-Discrimination-Policy.pdf (6 pages). Prohibits discrimination on the basis of caste, religion, gender, region, language, disability or any other protected characteristic, in admissions, academics, employment and campus life; provides redressal routes via the EOC and grievance committees.

### 23.10 Gender Equality Policy

**Policy:** Gender-Equality-Policy.pdf (5 pages). Commits to equal opportunity, gender-sensitive campus environment, women's safety (POSH/POCSO awareness), equal access to facilities, and representation in committees.

### 23.11 Library Policy

**Policy:** Library-policy.pdf (22 pages). Covers membership categories, borrowing entitlements and loan periods, renewals, overdue fines, loss and damage, reading-hall conduct, digital resource usage and licensing compliance, copyright and plagiarism, and library committee governance.

### 23.12 IT Policy

**Policy:** IT-Policy.pdf (47 pages — the most detailed institutional policy). Covers acceptable use of computing and network resources, campus Wi-Fi access, email and account management, data protection and privacy, information security, cybersecurity incident reporting, software licensing, hardware asset management, system access control, backup, and disciplinary consequences for misuse.

### 23.13 Cybersecurity Policy

Cybersecurity requirements are consolidated within the **IT Policy** (47 pages) covering network security, password and access control, malware protection, incident response, data privacy, safe internet use and reporting of security incidents to institutional IT support.

### 23.14 Green Campus Policy

**Policy:** Green-Campus-Policy.pdf (7 pages). Commits to sustainable campus operations — energy efficiency, renewable energy (the institution reports campus biogas and solar power facilities), water conservation, waste management and segregation, tree plantation and biodiversity, green purchasing, and environmental awareness; supported by the NSS eco-activities.

### 23.15 Internship Policy

**Policy:** INTERNSHIP-POLICY.pdf (19 pages). Defines internship objectives, duration and credit structure, industry/institute eligibility, student eligibility, approval workflow, internship agreement/MoU requirements, supervision and mentoring, assessment and reporting, stipend norms, safety and insurance, and documentation.

### 23.16 IPR Policy

**Policy:** IPR-Policy.pdf (7 pages). Defines intellectual property ownership (institution, inventor, sponsor shares), invention disclosure procedure, patent filing support, technology transfer and licensing, revenue sharing, confidentiality and non-disclosure, and the IPR committee.

### 23.17 NEP 2020 Policy

**Policy:** NEP-2020-Policy.pdf (5 pages). Sets out the university's implementation of the National Education Policy 2020 — multidisciplinary education, flexible credit-based pathways, multiple entry/exit, ABC (Academic Bank of Credits) alignment, skill and vocational integration, research and innovation emphasis, use of technology in learning, and holistic student development. The institution reports NEP 2020 Policy alignment in its AQAR disclosures including the possibility of offering vocational courses through ODL mode.

### 23.18 Academic Regulations

Academic regulations are issued per regulation code (MR15, MR17, MR18, MR20, MR21, MR22, MR24, MR25) and approved by the Academic Council. They define programme structure and credits, course categories, evaluation and grading (CBCS/OBE), attendance requirements, promotion and backlog rules, examination conduct, and degree award requirements. Published through the Academic Council page and department Syllabus tabs.

### 23.19 Research Policy

Published at /research/policies/research-policy, with the associated Consultancy Policy, Code of Ethics, Incentives Policy and Seed Grant Policy. The research policy framework covers research governance (R&D Cell, Research Advisory Board), scholar admission and supervision, publication and ethics standards, funding and seed grants, consultancy, IP and technology transfer, and research incentive structures.

### 23.20 Student Counselling Policy

**Policy:** Student Counselling Policy (12 pages) plus the About Student Counselling Cell document (4 pages) and Student Counsellor Committee list. Defines confidentiality, counselling scope, referral pathways, record-keeping, counsellor roles and the student-mentor system.

### 23.21 Equal Opportunity Cell Policy

**Policy:** EOC-Policy.pdf (3 pages) plus the Equal Opportunity Cell Committee document (3 pages). Mandates equitable access to academic resources, scholarships and facilities, and coordinates diversity and inclusion awareness.

### 23.22 Alumni Policy

**Policy:** Alumni-Policy.pdf (11 pages). Covers the constitution and objectives of the Alumni Association, registration, database maintenance, alumni engagement in curriculum revision and institutional governance, events and reunions, scholarships and fundraising, and alumni mentorship.

Scholarship notifications are announced each academic year on the notifications page; institutional scholarships (merit, sports/culture, government schemes) are described on /admissions/scholarships. Scholarship beneficiary data is disclosed in the AQAR reports.

### 25.10 Holiday Notifications

Holiday declarations and the university's list of holidays are published with the academic calendar (University Calendar 2026-27; academic year window 28 Jun 2026 – 02 Dec 2026) and through institutional circulars on the notices/notifications page.

### 25.11 Event Announcements

Event announcements are published on /events with status and department filters, and echoed in the notifications feed under the Events category (e.g., "IGNITE 2026 — Registration Open", 22 May 2026).

### 25.12 Circulars

Institutional circulars cover examinations (results, RC/RV, grace marks, exam instructions), academic matters (calendars, regulations), compliance (mandatory disclosure), and student matters (grievance portal, anti-ragging helpline). Circulars are published as PDFs under /uploads/2026/ and linked from the relevant section pages.

### 25.13 Important Deadlines

| Deadline type | Where published |
|---|---|
| Admission counselling / application deadlines | /notifications, /admissions, admissions office email |
| Exam fee last dates (with/without fine) | /examinations/fees (inside each PDF) |
| RC/RV application last dates | /examinations/circulars |
| Scholarship deadlines | /notifications, government portals |
| Event registration deadlines | /events |
| Ph.D. application deadlines | /phdadmissions, /research/phd |

### 25.14 Notification Data Schema

Every notification stored for the chatbot must carry: **Title, Category, Date, Description, Department, Programme, Semester, Deadline, PDF, Official URL, Status, Last Updated, Source**. Example:

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Title | Exam Fee Notification B.Tech and B.B.A Semester-III Regular Examination (MR25) |
| Category | Exams → Exam Fee Notification |
| Date | 12 Aug 2026 |
| Description | Examination fee notification for B.Tech and BBA Semester-III regular examination under regulation MR25 |
| Department | Examination Branch |
| Programme | B.Tech, BBA |
| Semester | III |
| Deadline | As stated inside the fee notification PDF |
| PDF | Published on /examinations/fees |
| Official URL | https://mrdu.edu.in/examinations/fees |
| Status | Active |
| Last Updated | 12 Aug 2026 |
| Source | Official MRDU website |

# PART 12 — Archives

*Checklist sections 26 — official MRDU sources.*

## 26. ARCHIVES

### 26.1 Purpose of the Archive

MRDU maintains legacy content from the MREC Autonomous era alongside MRDU-cycle content. The archive is critical for the chatbot because it explains why documents and regulations from earlier years reference "MREC" rather than "MRDU", and why older regulation codes (MR15–MR22) remain active for backlog students.

### 26.2 Governance Archives (published)

| Archive | Range | Source page |
|---|---|---|
| Academic Council meetings | 1st to 20th (MREC Autonomous) | /about/academic-council |
| Executive Council meetings | 1st to 15th (MREC Autonomous) | /about/executive-council |
| Finance Committee meetings | 1st to 15th (MREC Autonomous) | /about/finance-committee |
| Academic Council Reconstitution Charter | 2022–2023 | /about/academic-council |
| Executive Council Reconstitution Charter | 2022–2023 | /about/executive-council |
| Finance Committee Reconstitution Charter | 2022–2023 | /about/finance-committee |

These archives preserve the statutory history of the institution during its autonomous college phase, before the deemed university status of 2025.

### 26.3 Old News (archive records)

| 12 Jul 2025 | 20th Graduation, Placement & Alumni Day at MREC |

### 26.5 Old Notifications

Historical notifications are retained on /notifications and the section pages (for example "B.Tech Lateral Entry Admissions 2026–2027" from 01 Feb 2026 as a news item, and the 2026 exam fee notifications from March 2026). Notification history is essential to answer "what did the university announce about X?" with the correct date.

### 26.6 Old Placements

Historical placement records appear in the **AQAR reports** (2020-21, 2021-22, 2022-23, 2023-24), each containing the "Number of outgoing students who got placement during the year" and related placement metrics. The **SSR Cycle III** aggregates five years of placement performance.

### 26.7 Faculty Directory (Archive Note)

The current faculty directory holds 449 records. Former faculty and historical department compositions are archived in the AQAR/SSR faculty lists (which tabulate faculty by department, designation, qualification and experience for each assessment year).

### 26.8 Old Academic Calendars

| Academic Year | Calendars published |
|---|---|
| 2026–2027 | B.Tech I–IV Year; M.Tech I–II Year; MBA I–II Year; BBA I–II Year; B.Com & B.Sc I Year |
| 2025–2026 | B.Tech I–IV Year; M.Tech I–II Year; MBA I–II Year; BBA I–III Year |

Older academic calendars are retained in the institutional document repository and are referenced in AQAR disclosures (each AQAR states whether an academic calendar was prepared for the year).

### 26.9 Old Exam Notifications

Historical exam notifications include the April 2026 and February 2026 cycles (see Section 10.4). Retained timetables span regulations MR15 through MR25, which is precisely what makes the archive useful for students clearing old backlogs.

### 26.10 Old Results

Previous result circulars are retained on the examination circulars page and the university's /uploads/ archive (MRDU-RESULT CIRCULAR.pdf, Results-circular-Sem1.pdf and their predecessors).

### 26.11 Previous Admission Notifications

Admission notifications from earlier cycles remain accessible from the notifications page. The B.Tech Lateral Entry 2026–2027 notification and the Ph.D. Admissions 2025/2026 notifications are examples of retained admission-cycle documents.

### 26.12 Previous Circulars

Circular history includes exam circulars (results, RC/RV, grace marks, instructions), UGC circulars adopted by the institution (e.g., UGC Circular on Anti-Ragging Measures, 10 June 2025), and administrative circulars.

### 26.13 Previous Placement Reports

Previous placement performance is published through the AQAR series and the SSR Cycle III. A formal annual placement report is available from the Training & Placement Cell on request.

# PART 14 — Alumni

*Checklist sections 29 — official MRDU sources.*

## 29. ALUMNI

### 29.1 Alumni Association

"The college has constituted an Alumni Association to establish and strengthen close relations between its alumni and the institution. The association enables graduates to remain stakeholders in revising the vision and mission of the college and to participate in graduation ceremonies, technical fests, and exhibitions."

"The association maintains and updates pertinent information about alumni in its database, serves as a forum for exchange of ideas on academic, cultural, and social matters, and organizes reunion activities while supporting scholarships and fundraising initiatives."

**Page:** https://mrdu.edu.in/alumni

**Alumni Policy:** Alumni-Policy.pdf (11 pages).

### 29.2 Alumni Association Vision and Mission

**Vision:** "To foster a quality-centric culture within the institution by institutionalizing and internalizing all initiatives with the support of both internal and external stakeholders."

**Mission:** "To streamline and systematize institutional efforts towards achieving academic excellence and to serve as the driving force in enhancing quality by addressing deficiencies."

### 29.3 Alumni Registration

Alumni register with the Alumni Association through the alumni page ("Join the alumni network"). The association maintains an alumni database used for engagement, events, mentorship, placement referrals and institutional development.

### 29.4 Alumni Coordinators (department-wise)

- Reunions and technical fest participation organised by the Alumni Association

### 29.6 Distinguished Alumni (published)

| Alumnus | Achievement |
|---|---|

| Aravind Krishna | Biotech alumnus (2003–07 batch). Actor in Tollywood and sports person; participated successfully in a basketball league hosted by CB Japan. |
| Alumni-led solar desalination research | Nano–micro hierarchical structures enhancing solar desalination efficiency — alumni-led research contribution. |

### 29.7 Alumni Profiles

Alumni profiles are maintained in the Alumni Association database and feature in graduation day publications and institutional communications. Alumni achievements are published on the alumni page under "Alumni Achievements" by year (2023–2024 examples above).

### 29.8 Alumni Achievements

Published under two-year headings on the alumni page; the 2023–2024 block lists the Tollywood/sports alumnus, the solar desalination research contribution, and the Times Square music recognition.

### 29.9 Alumni Network

The network spans graduates of MREC/MREC(A) and MRDU across India and abroad (USA, Japan and other countries represented in published alumnus stories), connected through departmental coordinators and the central Alumni Association.

### 29.10 Alumni Testimonials

Testimonials from alumni appear in institutional communications, graduation day publications and the university's social channels. For specific verified testimonials, refer to the official alumni page and university social media.

### 29.11 Alumni Contributions

Alumni contributions include scholarship support and fundraising initiatives (per the official alumni description: "supporting scholarships and fundraising initiatives"), guest lectures, mentorship, industry referrals and participation in curriculum revision processes.

### 29.12 Alumni Mentorship

The Alumni Policy provides for alumni mentorship of current students — industry mentoring, career guidance, and participation in the Board of Studies/stakeholder feedback mechanisms used for curriculum design and review.

### 29.13 Alumni Placement Stories

Placement success stories featuring alumni are published through the placements page, the news section, and social media channels. The **21st Graduation, Placement and Alumni Day** combines graduation, placement recognition and alumni engagement in a single institutional event.
